      // process group as the main process, so a kill of the
      // process group would take Chrome down with it. We do not
      // rely on that (tree-kill handles the tree explicitly), but
      // keeping Chrome attached is the safer default.
      detached: false,
      // `windowsHide: true` — on Windows, do not open a console
      // window for the spawned process. Chrome is a GUI app and
      // does not need one; without this flag, some Windows
      // configurations briefly flash a console window on launch.
      windowsHide: true,
    });
  } catch (err) {
    if (portWasAllocated) {
      releasePort(port);
    }
    throw new Error(
      `Failed to spawn Chrome at ${executablePath}: ${
        err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)
      }`
    );
  }

  // ---- Wait for readiness ----
  const readyTimeoutMs = options.readyTimeoutMs ?? 30_000;
  try {
    await waitForDebugPort(port, readyTimeoutMs);
  } catch (err) {
    // Chrome did not open its debug port. Kill whatever was
    // spawned (it may be alive but stuck) and release the port.
    try {
      await treeKillAsync(child);
    } catch {
      // The kill failure does not change the outcome — Chrome is
      // not usable regardless. The original error is what the
      // caller needs to see.
    }
    if (portWasAllocated) {
      releasePort(port);
    }
    throw err;
  }

  // ---- Register and return ----
  const instance: ChromeInstance = {
    process: child,
    port,
    userDataDir: options.userDataDir,
    executablePath,
  };

  runningInstances.push(instance);

  // ---- Auto-remove on unexpected exit ----
  // If Chrome exits on its own (a crash, a user closing the window
  // manually), the instance should not linger in the registry
  // holding a port. The `exit` event fires for a clean exit AND a
  // crash (with a non-zero code), so a single handler covers both.
  //
  // The handler does NOT call `killChrome` — the process is already
  // dead, and running tree-kill on a dead process is a no-op that
  // would only slow the cleanup down. It removes the registry
  // entry directly and releases the port.
  child.once('exit', () => {
    const idx = runningInstances.indexOf(instance);
    if (idx !== -1) {
      runningInstances.splice(idx, 1);
      releasePort(instance.port);
    }
  });

  return instance;
};

// =============================================================================
//  Public API — kill
// =============================================================================

/**
 * Terminate a Chrome instance and release its port.
 *
 * Uses `tree-kill` to terminate the whole process tree, not just
 * the root Chrome process — see the file header for why this is
 * required on Windows. Waits for the kill to complete (tree-kill's
 * callback is wrapped in a promise) so that callers can await this
 * and then safely release the port.
 *
 * If the instance has already been killed (its `process` field is
 * null, or it is not in the registry), this function is a no-op.
 * This makes cleanup paths — which often run on both success and
 * failure — idempotent without needing an explicit "was I already
 * killed" check.
 *
 * The instance's entry in the registry is removed as part of the
 * kill. The port is released back to the allocator's pool.
 *
 * @param instance - The handle returned by `launchChrome`.
 */
export const killChrome = async (instance: ChromeInstance): Promise<void> => {
  // ---- Registry bookkeeping ----
  // Remove the instance from the registry FIRST so that a
  // concurrent `killAllChrome` does not try to kill it a second
  // time. Both functions check the registry membership before
  // killing, so removing here is what makes the two paths
  // mutually exclusive for a given instance.
  const idx = runningInstances.indexOf(instance);
  if (idx !== -1) {
    runningInstances.splice(idx, 1);
  }

  // ---- Kill the process tree ----
  if (instance.process !== null) {
    try {
      await treeKillAsync(instance.process);
    } catch {
      // A kill failure is logged but does not prevent the port
      // release. The port will be freed for the next allocation
      // either way; a Chrome that survived a tree-kill is a rare
      // and system-level problem, not something this function can
      // meaningfully recover from.
    }
  }

  // ---- Release the port ----
  // Idempotent at the allocator layer — a port that is not
  // currently in use is silently ignored by `releasePort`. This
  // is what makes the double-kill path (manual kill, then
  // shutdown's killAllChrome) safe.
  releasePort(instance.port);
};

/**
 * Terminate every tracked Chrome instance.
 *
 * Iterates the registry from the end backward (most-recently-
 * launched first) and awaits each kill. Called from the app's
 * `before-quit` handler so that quitting the app does not leave
 * orphaned Chrome processes on the user's machine.
 *
 * The registry is fully cleared by the end of this call, whether
 * every kill succeeded or not. A Chrome process that survived a
 * tree-kill is a system-level anomaly; it is not a reason to
 * leave the registry in an inconsistent state.
 *
 * Safe to call when no instances are running — an empty registry
 * iterates zero times.
 */
export const killAllChrome = async (): Promise<void> => {
  // Snapshot the registry and clear it up front, so that any
  // concurrent `launchChrome` call during the loop does not have
  // its new instance killed by this shutdown path.
  const snapshot = runningInstances.splice(0, runningInstances.length);

  // Kill in reverse order — the most-recently-launched first.
  for (let i = snapshot.length - 1; i >= 0; i -= 1) {
    const instance = snapshot[i];
    if (!instance) continue;
    try {
      if (instance.process !== null) {
        await treeKillAsync(instance.process);
      }
    } catch {
      // Best-effort. See `killChrome` for the same reasoning.
    }
    releasePort(instance.port);
  }
};

// =============================================================================
//  Public API — introspection
// =============================================================================

/**
 * Return a snapshot of the currently-tracked instances.
 *
 * The returned array is a fresh copy on every call. The caller
 * cannot mutate the registry by mutating the array, and the
 * returned array does not change if instances are added or
 * removed after the call. Both are what a caller wants when
 * inspecting state for a diagnostic log line or a test assertion.
 *
 * Exported for diagnostics and tests. Production code paths
 * generally hold their own handle (returned by `launchChrome`) and
 * do not need to query the registry.
 */
export const getRunningInstances = (): readonly ChromeInstance[] => {
  return [...runningInstances];
};

// =============================================================================
//  Internal — tree-kill wrapper
// =============================================================================

/**
 * Wrap `tree-kill`'s callback API in a promise.
 *
 * `tree-kill` accepts a callback `(err?: Error) => void`. This
 * wrapper resolves on success and rejects on error, so callers can
 * use `await treeKillAsync(child)` instead of managing the
 * callback.
 *
 * The wrapper resolves if the child has no PID — a `ChildProcess`
 * whose `spawn` failed synchronously has `pid === undefined`, and
 * there is nothing to kill. This mirrors the behavior the caller
 * would want: a kill of an already-dead process is a success, not
 * an error.
 */
const treeKillAsync = (child: ChildProcess): Promise<void> => {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const pid = child.pid;
    if (pid === undefined) {
      resolve();
      return;
    }
    treeKill(pid, (err?: Error) => {
      if (err) {
        reject(err);
      } else {
        resolve();
      }
    });
  });
};
</file>

<file path="apps/desktop/src/features/research/services/searchService.ts">
/**
 * searchService — orchestrates one search operation.
 *
 * A search is a multi-part dance between four modules:
 *
 *   portAllocator        → gives Chrome a debug port
 *   chromeLauncher       → ensures a Chrome instance is running on that port
 *   childProcessManager  → spawns `python main.py search ...`
 *   listenerBridge       → (NOT USED — see the note below)
 *
 * This module composes them, exposes a single `runSearch()` entry
 * point, and returns a candidate list (or a structured failure) to
 * the caller.
 *
 * ── The flow ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
 *
 *   1. Ensure Chrome is running. Reuse the app's existing
 *      persistent-profile Chrome if one is registered with the
 *      launcher; otherwise launch a fresh one with the app's
 *      chrome-profile path. Chrome is NOT killed at the end of
 *      the operation — it lives for the app's lifetime so that
 *      the user's DeepSeek login (and every other cookie in the
 *      profile) survives across operations. Shutdown is the
 *      app's `before-quit` handler's job.
 *
 *   2. Spawn `python main.py search` with the operation's
 *      arguments and the Chrome port. The task ID is derived
 *      from the topic ID (`${topicId}-search`) so that a cancel
 *      call from the renderer can find the manager.
 *
 *   3. Subscribe to the manager's `stdout` event. Each line is
 *      parsed as a `::ROOKIE::<TYPE>::<json>` marker; a parsed
 *      marker is broadcast to every renderer window over the
 *      `research:marker` IPC channel — the same channel the
 *      listener bridge uses. SEARCH_PROGRESS markers drive the
 *      progress UI; SEARCH_RESULT carries the candidate list;
 *      CAPTCHA_REQUIRED opens the modal; TASK_START / TASK_END
 *      bracket the operation.
 *
 *   4. Wait for the child to exit. The exit code classifies the
 *      outcome:
 *
 *        0   → success. Return whatever candidates the
 *              SEARCH_RESULT marker carried (or an empty list if
 *              the query genuinely matched nothing).
 *        99  → the user clicked Skip. Return kind='user-skip'.
 *        100 → the user clicked Stop. Return kind='user-stop'.
 *        101 → the captcha policy was 'skip' and a captcha
 *              appeared. Return kind='user-skip'.
 *        any other non-zero → a generic failure. Return
 *              kind='error' with the process's exit code in the
 *              message.
 *
 *   5. If the primary engine failed for a recoverable reason
 *      (no candidates, a generic error — NOT a user skip/stop),
 *      and the caller did not opt out of fallback, retry the
 *      whole flow with `--engine duckduckgo` on the same Chrome.
 *
 *   6. On success, write the final candidate list to the topic's
 *      `candidates.json` file. The write is a best-effort cache
 *      step — a failure is logged but does not change the
 *      result. See the "Persistence" bullet in "What this module
 *      does NOT do" for the file's shape and purpose.
 *
 * Dependencies: Node stdlib + electron + the three sibling
 *
 * ── The marker-routing design tradeoff ──────────────────────────
 *
 *   `listenerBridge.ts` was written to attach to a raw
 *   `ChildProcess` object. `childProcessManager.ts` does not
 *   expose its internal `ChildProcess` — its `process` field is
 *   private, and its `start()` method returns only a resolved
 *   `Promise<void>`. So this module cannot pass the child to
 *   `attachToProcess()`.
 *
 *   The alternative was to modify one of the two files, which the
 *   current session forbids. Instead, this module attaches its
 *   own listener to the manager's `stdout` events, parses markers
 *   with a small local parser (same algorithm as the bridge's),
 *   and broadcasts them itself using the same
 *   `MARKER_EVENT_CHANNEL` constant and the same
 *   `webContents.send` shape.
 *
 *   The renderer cannot tell the difference — it receives the
 *   same `{ type, payload }` events on the same channel it would
 *   have received from the bridge. The duplication is roughly
 *   twenty lines of parsing logic. If a future session decides
 *   the manager should expose a public getter for its raw
 *   process (or if `listenerBridge` is redesigned to accept a
 *   manager instead of a child), this module can switch to the
 *   bridge in one edit.
 *
 * ── What this module does NOT do ────────────────────────────────
 *
 *   - It does not kill Chrome. Chrome is the app's shared,
 *     persistent-profile instance; killing it at the end of every
 *     search would force a re-login to DeepSeek on the next
 *     operation. Shutdown is `main/index.ts`'s `before-quit`
 *     handler's job.
 *
 *   - It does not retry within a single engine. The "retries"
 *     the plan's annotation refers to are the fallback to a
 *     different engine — see step 5. A network error that would
 *     resolve on retry with the same engine is not currently
 *     handled; adding it would require a backoff loop and a
 *     distinguished error signal from the Python script.
 *
 *   - It does not open the candidate pages. Fetching each
 *     candidate's content is `extractionService.ts`'s job
 *     (Session 12).
 *
 *   - It does not filter or rank candidates. The list is returned
 *     in the order Google or DuckDuckGo ranked it; the renderer's
 *     SourceList renders the list and the user decides.
 *
 *   - It does not persist to the backend. A successful search
 *     writes the candidate list to `<topicFolder>/candidates.json`
 *     so a returning visit to the topic can repopulate the
 *     Sources tab without re-running the search. The backend's
 *     `/api/topics` and `/api/documents` endpoints remain the
 *     persistence layer for the user's topic and document
 *     records; the `candidates.json` file is a per-topic cache
 *     that the research feature owns.
 *
 *   - It does not write `candidates.json` with more than one
 *     populated bucket in this session. The current search runs
 *     one query against one engine and returns one list; all of
 *     that list lands under the `anytime` bucket, and the other
 *     three buckets are empty. The bucketed-search feature (PR-3
 *     of the plan) changes this file to route each per-bucket
 *     search's result into its own slot.
 *
 * Dependencies: Node stdlib + electron + the three sibling
 * services (`portAllocator` is used indirectly via
 * `chromeLauncher`).
 */

import { app, BrowserWindow } from 'electron';
import { promises as fs } from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';

import {
  MARKER_EVENT_CHANNEL,
  type MarkerEvent,
} from './listenerBridge.js';
import {
  getRunningInstances,
  launchChrome,
} from './chromeLauncher.js';
import {
  createProcessManager,
  disposeProcessManager,
  type ProcessOptions,
} from './childProcessManager.js';

// =============================================================================
//  Public types
// =============================================================================

/**
 * The two search engines the pipeline can drive. The same union
 * the Python side's `main.py` accepts via its `--engine` flag.
 */
export type SearchEngine = 'google' | 'duckduckgo';

/**
 * One candidate source page, as returned by the Python search
 * script and forwarded to the renderer. Every field is a plain
 * string — the Python side guarantees they are present and
 * non-empty for every candidate it emits.
 */
export interface SearchCandidate {
  /** The absolute URL of the page. */
  url: string;

  /** The page's title, as displayed on the SERP. */
  title: string;

  /** The SERP snippet — a one- or two-line preview of the page's
   *  content. May be an empty string for results that do not carry
   *  one. */
  snippet: string;

  /** The platform slug for the URL's domain — `'reddit'`,
   *  `'quora'`, `'medium'`, `'twitter'`, or `'generic'`. */
  platform: string;
}

/**
 * The four time-range buckets the bucketed search queries. The
 * values match the strings the Python search scripts accept on
 * their `--time-range` argument (see `research/google_search.py`).
 *
 * The union is declared here — in the main-process service — so
 * that the IPC handler (`researchIPC.ts`) and any future
 * main-process consumer have a single authoritative shape to
 * reference. The renderer's research store declares its own copy
 * of the same union because the renderer cannot import from a
 * main-process module; the two declarations must stay in sync.
 */
export type BucketKey = 'anytime' | 'year' | 'month' | 'week';

/**
 * A topic's candidate list, split by time-range bucket. The
 * shape is what `candidates.json` carries on disk and what the
 * `research:getCandidates` IPC channel returns to the renderer.
 *
 * Every bucket key is present; a bucket the search has not yet
 * run holds an empty array. The current session (before PR-3
 * ships the bucketed-search feature) writes the entire candidate
 * list under `anytime` and leaves the other three buckets empty.
 * PR-3 changes the write to route each per-bucket search's
 * result into its own slot.
 */
export type CandidatesByBucket = Record<BucketKey, SearchCandidate[]>;

/**
 * The classification of a search's outcome.
 *
 *   'success'       — the search ran and found at least one
 *                     candidate. `success` is `true`.
 *   'no-candidates' — the search ran cleanly but found nothing.
 *                     `success` is `false`.
 *   'error'         — the search process failed for a reason other
 *                     than a user action. `success` is `false`.
 *   'user-skip'     — the user clicked Skip on a blocking prompt,
 *                     or the operation was cancelled. `success` is
 *                     `false`.
 *   'user-stop'     — the user clicked Stop. `success` is `false`.
 */
export type SearchResultKind =
  | 'success'
  | 'no-candidates'
  | 'error'
  | 'user-skip'
  | 'user-stop';

/**
 * The options `runSearch` accepts.
 */
export interface SearchServiceOptions {
  /** The search query — normally the topic's title. */
  query: string;

  /** The user's keywords. Carried in the marker payload for
   *  display, but does NOT affect the search itself. */
  keywords: string[];

  /** The maximum number of candidates to collect. The search may
   *  return fewer if the engine runs out of results. */
  maxResults: number;

  /** The topic's ID. Used to derive the task ID (so the cancel
   *  path can find the manager) and included in every emitted
   *  marker so the renderer routes the candidates to the right
   *  store entry. */
  topicId: string;

  /** Which engine to try first. Defaults to `'google'`. */
  engine?: SearchEngine;

  /** Whether to fall back to DuckDuckGo if Google fails.
   *  Defaults to `true`. Setting it to `false` makes a Google
   *  failure final — useful in tests that need determinism, and
   *  in a future UI that lets the user lock an engine. */
  fallbackToDuckDuckGo?: boolean;

  /** The captcha policy to forward to the Python script. See
   *  `ProcessOptions.captchaPolicy` for the semantics. Omit to
   *  get the Python side's default of `'ask'`. */
  captchaPolicy?: 'ask' | 'skip' | 'stop';
}

/**
 * The result of one `runSearch` call. Never thrown — the promise
 * always resolves, and failures are described by the `success` /
 * `kind` / `error` fields.
 */
export interface SearchServiceResult {
  /** True only when `kind === 'success'`. */
  success: boolean;

  /** The candidate list the search produced. Populated on success,
   *  and possibly populated on failure if the search had collected
   *  candidates before failing — the caller can decide whether to
   *  keep them. */
  candidates: SearchCandidate[];

  /** Which engine produced this result. Under a successful fallback
   *  this is `'duckduckgo'` even though the caller requested
   *  Google; the value reflects the engine that actually ran. */
  engine: SearchEngine;

  /** The classification of the outcome. See `SearchResultKind`. */
  kind: SearchResultKind;

  /** A human-readable error message. Present whenever `success`
   *  is `false`. */
  error?: string;
}

// =============================================================================
//  Constants
// =============================================================================

/** The engine tried first when the caller does not specify one. */
const DEFAULT_ENGINE: SearchEngine = 'google';

/** The engine tried if the primary engine fails. */
const FALLBACK_ENGINE: SearchEngine = 'duckduckgo';

/** The marker prefix that every Python script writes on stdout.
 *  Must match `core/output.py` and `listenerBridge.ts`. */
const MARKER_PREFIX = '::ROOKIE::';

/** The exit code the Python side uses for "user clicked Skip"
 *  (mirrors `core/output.py`'s `EXIT_SKIPPED`). */
const EXIT_SKIPPED = 99;

/** The exit code for "user clicked Stop" (`EXIT_STOPPED`). */
const EXIT_STOPPED = 100;

/** The exit code for "auto-skipped because captcha policy was
 *  'skip'" (`EXIT_CAPTCHA_SKIP`). */
const EXIT_CAPTCHA_SKIP = 101;

// ---- On-disk layout ----
//
// The topic folder convention. Same values every other main-process
// module that touches the topic folder uses (`writerIPC.ts`,
// `validationIPC.ts`, `documentService.ts`). Duplicated across
// files because the convention is a cross-file contract and the
// plan's tree declares no shared layout module for the desktop
// main process. A change here must be reflected in the sibling
// files and on the Python side in the same commit.

/** The app slug used as the top-level folder under the user's
 *  application-data directory. Must match the Electron side's
 *  `main/store.ts` slug and the Python side's
 *  `core/file_io._APP_SLUG`. */
const APP_SLUG = 'writespeare';

/** The subfolder under the app slug that holds every topic. */
const TOPICS_SUBFOLDER = 'topics';

/** The filename of the per-topic candidate list, written by
 *  `writeCandidatesFile` after a successful search. */
const CANDIDATES_FILE = 'candidates.json';

// =============================================================================
//  Helpers
// =============================================================================

/**
 * The absolute path to the persistent Chrome profile the app
 * uses. Deterministic across operations so that every Chrome
 * launched by the app shares one profile — and one DeepSeek login.
 *
 * The path is `<userData>/chrome-profile`. On Windows that
 * resolves to `%APPDATA%/writespeare-desktop/chrome-profile`; on
 * macOS to `~/Library/Application Support/writespeare-desktop/chrome-profile`;
 * on Linux to `~/.config/writespeare-desktop/chrome-profile`. The
 * `userData` name is set by Electron from the app's package.json
 * `name` field.
 */
const getChromeProfilePath = (): string => {
  return path.join(app.getPath('userData'), 'chrome-profile');
};

/**
 * Return the current app-wide Chrome instance's debug port,
 * launching a fresh Chrome if none is running.
 *
 * See the file header for why Chrome is reused across operations
 * rather than launched per operation — the persistent profile
 * holds the user's DeepSeek login and killing Chrome would force
 * a re-login.
 *
 * The function is `async` because launching Chrome (the slow path)
 * is async. The fast path — an existing instance — returns
 * synchronously wrapped in a resolved promise.
 */
const ensureChromeRunning = async (): Promise<number> => {
  const running = getRunningInstances();
  const first = running[0];
  if (first !== undefined) {
    return first.port;
  }
  const instance = await launchChrome({
    userDataDir: getChromeProfilePath(),
  });
  return instance.port;
};

/**
 * Parse a single `::ROOKIE::<TYPE>::<json>` line into a
 * `MarkerEvent`, or return `null` for a non-marker line.
 *
 * Same algorithm as `listenerBridge.ts`'s private parser. The
 * duplication is documented in the file header — it exists
 * because the bridge's parser is not exported.
 */
const parseMarkerLine = (line: string): MarkerEvent | null => {
  if (!line.startsWith(MARKER_PREFIX)) {
    return null;
  }
  const afterPrefix = line.slice(MARKER_PREFIX.length);
  const typeEnd = afterPrefix.indexOf('::');
  if (typeEnd === -1) {
    return null;
  }
  const type = afterPrefix.slice(0, typeEnd);
  if (type.length === 0) {
    return null;
  }
  const jsonText = afterPrefix.slice(typeEnd + 2);
  let payload: unknown;
  try {
    payload = jsonText.length > 0 ? JSON.parse(jsonText) : {};
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
  return { type, payload };
};

/**
 * Broadcast a parsed marker to every renderer window.
 *
 * Same shape and same channel as `listenerBridge.broadcast` — the
 * renderer cannot tell which module emitted a given marker.
 */
const broadcastMarker = (event: MarkerEvent): void => {
  const windows = BrowserWindow.getAllWindows();
  for (const win of windows) {
    if (win.isDestroyed()) continue;
    win.webContents.send(MARKER_EVENT_CHANNEL, event);
  }
};

/**
 * Extract the candidate list from a `SEARCH_RESULT` marker's
 * payload, narrowing the opaque `unknown` to `SearchCandidate[]`
 * with runtime checks.
 *
 * A malformed candidate entry — a non-object, a missing `url`, a
 * missing `title` — is skipped rather than included with default
 * values. The Python side is expected to emit well-formed
 * payloads; the guard exists so a bug in the Python side produces
 * a smaller list, not a crash in the main process.
 */
const extractCandidates = (payload: unknown): SearchCandidate[] => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) {
    return [];
  }
  const candidatesField = (payload as { candidates?: unknown }).candidates;
  if (!Array.isArray(candidatesField)) {
    return [];
  }

  const result: SearchCandidate[] = [];
  for (const raw of candidatesField) {
    if (typeof raw !== 'object' || raw === null) continue;
    const c = raw as Record<string, unknown>;
    const url = c['url'];
    const title = c['title'];
    if (typeof url !== 'string' || url.length === 0) continue;
    if (typeof title !== 'string' || title.length === 0) continue;
    const snippet = typeof c['snippet'] === 'string' ? c['snippet'] : '';
    const platform =
      typeof c['platform'] === 'string' ? c['platform'] : 'generic';
    result.push({ url, title, snippet, platform });
  }
  return result;
};

/**
 * Return the absolute path to a topic's `candidates.json` file.
 *
 * The path is `<appData>/writespeare/topics/<topicId>/candidates.json`.
 * Same folder convention every other main-process module uses;
 * see the layout constants above for the duplication rationale.
 */
const getCandidatesPath = (topicId: string): string => {
  return path.join(
    app.getPath('appData'),
    APP_SLUG,
    TOPICS_SUBFOLDER,
    topicId,
    CANDIDATES_FILE
  );
};

/**
 * Write the candidate list to the topic's `candidates.json` file
 * in the `CandidatesByBucket` shape.
 *
 * The file is the persistence layer for the Sources tab: without
 * it, a returning visit to the topic would show an empty
 * candidate list even though the search had run. The
 * `research:getCandidates` IPC channel reads this file and
 * returns its contents to the renderer's `loadCandidates` action.
 *
 * In this session, all candidates land under the `anytime`
 * bucket and the other three buckets are empty — the bucketed
 * search does not exist yet. PR-3 of the plan changes this
 * helper to write into the bucket the search ran against.
 *
 * The parent directory is created recursively before the write
 * so the first search on a fresh topic succeeds without a
 * separate folder-creation step. A failure to write is
 * propagated to the caller, which logs it and continues — see
 * `runSearch`'s tail for the caller's policy.
 */
const writeCandidatesFile = async (
  topicId: string,
  candidates: SearchCandidate[]
): Promise<void> => {
  const candidatesPath = getCandidatesPath(topicId);
  await fs.mkdir(path.dirname(candidatesPath), { recursive: true });

  const record: CandidatesByBucket = {
    anytime: candidates,
    year: [],
    month: [],
    week: [],
  };

  await fs.writeFile(
    candidatesPath,
    JSON.stringify(record, null, 2),
    'utf-8'
  );
};

/**
 * The deterministic task ID for a search operation on a given
 * topic. Exported so that the IPC handler's cancel path can
 * compute the same ID without duplicating the naming convention.
 *
 * The task ID is what `createProcessManager` and
 * `disposeProcessManager` (and the manager registry) key on, so
 * the cancel handler and the service must agree on it exactly.
 */
export const getSearchTaskId = (topicId: string): string => {
  return `${topicId}-search`;
};

// =============================================================================
//  Internal — single-engine attempt
// =============================================================================

/**
 * Run one search on one engine and resolve when the child either
 * emits a terminal marker or exits.
 *
 * Never rejects — the returned promise always resolves, and the
 * caller distinguishes success from failure via the result's
 * `kind` field. The pattern matches how the renderer's stores
 * consume results: a plain object with a `success` boolean is
 * easier to reason about than a promise that might throw.
 */
const attemptSearch = async (
  options: SearchServiceOptions,
  engine: SearchEngine,
  port: number
): Promise<SearchServiceResult> => {
  const taskId = getSearchTaskId(options.topicId);

  // Dispose any manager that survived a previous operation on
  // this topic. A stale manager could happen if the previous
  // search crashed without going through `settle`, or if the
  // caller cancelled but did not dispose. Disposing is a no-op
  // when no manager exists for this task ID.
  disposeProcessManager(taskId);

  const manager = createProcessManager(taskId);

  return new Promise<SearchServiceResult>((resolve) => {
    let settled = false;
    let lastCandidates: SearchCandidate[] = [];

    /**
     * Finalize the operation: mark as settled, dispose the
     * manager (which kills the child if it is still running),
     * resolve the promise. Idempotent — a second call is a no-op.
     *
     * The manager is disposed BEFORE the resolve because
     * disposing is what stops any pending child from continuing
     * to emit events into a promise that has already resolved.
     */
    const settle = (result: SearchServiceResult): void => {
      if (settled) return;
      settled = true;
      disposeProcessManager(taskId);
      resolve(result);
    };

    // ---- stdout handler ----
    // Each line that parses as a marker is broadcast to every
    // renderer window. SEARCH_RESULT markers also update the
    // local `lastCandidates` so the exit handler can return them.
    manager.on('stdout', (line: string) => {
      const marker = parseMarkerLine(line);
      if (marker === null) return;
      broadcastMarker(marker);
      if (marker.type === 'SEARCH_RESULT') {
        lastCandidates = extractCandidates(marker.payload);
      }
    });

    // ---- exit handler ----
    // The exit code classifies the outcome. `code` is `number |
    // null` — a null arrives when the process was terminated by a
    // signal rather than exiting on its own.
    manager.on('exit', (code: number | null, _signal: string | null) => {
      if (code === 0) {
        const kind: SearchResultKind =
          lastCandidates.length > 0 ? 'success' : 'no-candidates';
        settle({
          success: kind === 'success',
          candidates: lastCandidates,
          engine,
          kind,
          ...(kind === 'success'
            ? {}
            : { error: 'Search completed with no candidates.' }),
        });
        return;
      }
      if (code === EXIT_SKIPPED || code === EXIT_CAPTCHA_SKIP) {
        settle({
          success: false,
          candidates: lastCandidates,
          engine,
          kind: 'user-skip',
          error: 'Search skipped by user.',
        });
        return;
      }
      if (code === EXIT_STOPPED) {
        settle({
          success: false,
          candidates: lastCandidates,
          engine,
          kind: 'user-stop',
          error: 'Search stopped by user.',
        });
        return;
      }
      settle({
        success: false,
        candidates: lastCandidates,
        engine,
        kind: 'error',
        error: `Search process exited with code ${code ?? 'unknown'}.`,
      });
    });

    // ---- error handler ----
    // The manager's `error` event fires when the child fails to
    // spawn (ENOENT, EACCES) or encounters a runtime error.
    manager.on('error', (err: Error) => {
      settle({
        success: false,
        candidates: lastCandidates,
        engine,
        kind: 'error',
        error: err.message,
      });
    });

    // ---- timeout handler ----
    // Fires when the caller set a timeout and the child exceeded
    // it. `runSearch` does not set one by default, so this path
    // only fires if a future caller overrides the default.
    manager.on('timeout', () => {
      settle({
        success: false,
        candidates: lastCandidates,
        engine,
        kind: 'error',
        error: 'Search timed out.',
      });
    });

    // ---- killed handler ----
    // Fires when the manager's `kill()` terminates the child
    // directly (a hard cancel that bypasses the graceful
    // stdin-based Skip/Stop flow). Treating it as a user skip is
    // the correct semantic — the user asked for the operation to
    // end, and the operation ended.
    manager.on('killed', () => {
      settle({
        success: false,
        candidates: lastCandidates,
        engine,
        kind: 'user-skip',
        error: 'Search was cancelled.',
      });
    });

    // ---- Build the arguments ----
    // The Python subcommand takes the operation name, the
    // operation's inputs, and the debug port. `--control-mode`
    // is always passed — the whole point of this operation is
    // the marker stream that goes back to the renderer.
    const args: string[] = [
      'search',
      '--query', options.query,
      '--keywords', options.keywords.join(','),
      '--max-results', String(options.maxResults),
      '--topic-id', options.topicId,
      '--port', String(port),
      '--engine', engine,
      '--control-mode',
    ];

    const startOptions: ProcessOptions = { args };
    if (options.captchaPolicy !== undefined) {
      startOptions.captchaPolicy = options.captchaPolicy;
    }

    // ---- Spawn ----
    // `start()` resolves once the child is spawned. Its promise
    // can reject if the spawn call itself throws; the catch
    // converts a rejection into the same `settle` the error
    // handler uses, so the caller always sees a resolved result.
    void manager.start(startOptions).catch((err: unknown) => {
      settle({
        success: false,
        candidates: [],
        engine,
        kind: 'error',
        error: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
      });
    });
  });
};

// =============================================================================
//  Public entry point
// =============================================================================

/**
 * Run a search operation and return the candidate list, or a
 * structured failure.
 *
 * See the file header for the full flow. The function never
 * rejects — a failure is reported via the resolved result's
 * `success: false` and `kind` fields.
 *
 * The candidate list is returned in the order the search engine
 * ranked it. The caller is responsible for whatever filtering
 * or reordering it wants before showing the list to the user.
 */
export const runSearch = async (
  options: SearchServiceOptions
): Promise<SearchServiceResult> => {
  const primaryEngine: SearchEngine = options.engine ?? DEFAULT_ENGINE;

  // ---- Validate ----
  // Two guards, both of which would produce confusing downstream
  // errors if allowed through. An empty query cannot be searched;
  // an empty topic ID cannot be used as a task ID (the manager's
  // constructor rejects it with a less-clear message).
  if (options.query.trim().length === 0) {
    return {
      success: false,
      candidates: [],
      engine: primaryEngine,
      kind: 'error',
      error: 'Search query is empty.',
    };
  }
  if (options.topicId.trim().length === 0) {
    return {
      success: false,
      candidates: [],
      engine: primaryEngine,
      kind: 'error',
      error: 'Topic ID is required.',
    };
  }

  // ---- Ensure Chrome is running ----
  // The port is returned to the Python script as a CLI argument.
  // If Chrome cannot be launched, the whole operation fails
  // before any Python process is spawned — an early, clear
  // failure instead of a confusing "could not connect to port
  // NNNN" from the Python side.
  let port: number;
  try {
    port = await ensureChromeRunning();
  } catch (err) {
    return {
      success: false,
      candidates: [],
      engine: primaryEngine,
      kind: 'error',
      error: `Could not launch Chrome: ${
        err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)
      }`,
    };
  }

  // ---- Try the primary engine ----
  const primaryResult = await attemptSearch(
    options,
    primaryEngine,
    port
  );

  // ---- Decide whether to fall back ----
  // Fallback is skipped when any of the following holds:
  //   * the primary engine succeeded
  //   * the caller explicitly disabled it
  //   * the primary engine was not Google (nothing to fall back to)
  //   * the user already terminated the operation (skip / stop)
  //
  // The last case matters because a fallback would ignore the
  // user's intent: they clicked Skip, and the service must not
  // silently spawn a second engine.
  //
  // The whole decision is collapsed into a single `finalResult`
  // variable so that the success path has one place to write the
  // candidates file and one place to return. The alternative —
  // writing at each of the original four return points — would
  // duplicate the write call and let future edits forget one of
  // them.
  let finalResult: SearchServiceResult = primaryResult;
  if (
    primaryResult.kind !== 'success' &&
    options.fallbackToDuckDuckGo !== false &&
    primaryEngine === 'google' &&
    primaryResult.kind !== 'user-skip' &&
    primaryResult.kind !== 'user-stop'
  ) {
    console.log(
      `[searchService] Primary search failed (${primaryResult.kind}: ` +
        `${primaryResult.error ?? 'no error message'}). ` +
        `Falling back to DuckDuckGo.`
    );

    finalResult = await attemptSearch(
      options,
      FALLBACK_ENGINE,
      port
    );
  }

  // ---- Persist candidates on success ----
  // A successful search writes the candidate list to the topic's
  // `candidates.json` file so that a subsequent open of the topic
  // can repopulate the Sources tab without re-running the search.
  //
  // A write failure is logged but does NOT change the result. The
  // search itself succeeded; the renderer's SourceList shows the
  // candidates that arrived via the marker stream regardless of
  // whether they were persisted. A disk-full or permission-denied
  // failure would cost the persistence but not the operation, and
  // the correct response is to let the user see the results and
  // move on.
  if (finalResult.kind === 'success') {
    try {
      await writeCandidatesFile(options.topicId, finalResult.candidates);
    } catch (err) {
      console.error(
        '[searchService] could not write candidates.json:',
        err
      );
    }
  }

  return finalResult;
};

// =============================================================================
//  Default export
// =============================================================================

/**
 * Every export bundled under one default object, matching the
 * convention of the sibling service modules (`portAllocator`,
 * `chromeLauncher`, `childProcessManager`). A caller that prefers
 * named imports uses `import { runSearch } from ...`; a caller
 * that prefers the default form uses
 * `import searchService from ...` and destructures.
 */
export default {
  runSearch,
  getSearchTaskId,
};
</file>

<file path="apps/desktop/src/features/research/store/researchStore.ts">
/**
 * researchStore — the renderer's transient state for the research feature.
 *
 * Holds the state that drives the Sources and Documents tabs on
 * the topic-detail page:
 *
 *   candidatesByTopic      — per-topic bucket-keyed candidate lists.
 *                            Each topic's slot is a full
 *                            `CandidatesByBucket` record, one array
 *                            per time-range bucket.
 *   searchProgressByTopic  — per-topic per-bucket live search
 *                            progress; a bucket's slot is null when
 *                            not currently in flight.
 *   extractProgressByTopic — per-topic live extraction progress.
 *   extractedCountByTopic  — per-topic count of docs saved by the
 *                            last extraction.
 *   failedByTopic          — per-topic list of URLs whose extraction
 *                            failed, loaded from `failures.json`.
 *   statusByTopic          — per-topic lifecycle state.
 *   errorByTopic           — per-topic last error message, or null.
 *   activeTopicId          — the topic that most recently started a
 *                            search; tracking field only.
 *
 * And the actions the components call to drive those operations:
 *
 *   runFullSearch     — invoke the search IPC once per configured
 *                       bucket, sequentially; update per-bucket
 *                       progress as each bucket runs
 *   runExtraction     — invoke the extract IPC with the selected
 *                       URLs
 *   retryFailed       — re-run extraction for the URLs whose last
 *                       attempt failed
 *   cancelOperation   — cancel whatever is running for a topic
 *   loadCandidates    — read the topic's saved candidates and
 *                       failures from disk on first open
 *   clearCandidates   — empty one topic's candidate buckets
 *   clearError        — dismiss the last error for one topic
 *   reset             — return every field to its initial value
 *
 * ── How the store receives updates ────────────────────────────
 *
 *   The store does not poll. It does not hold an IPC listener per
 *   component. Instead, on module load, it installs ONE listener on
 *   the `research:marker` channel via the preload's generic
 *   `window.electronAPI.on(...)` API. Every marker the main
 *   process emits (from searchService, from extractionService, and
 *   later from the writer and validation services) flows through
 *   that one listener; the store's handler routes it to the state
 *   field the marker type maps to.
 *
 *   The one-listener design is deliberate. The alternative — each
 *   component that needs progress data attaches its own listener —
 *   would create as many listeners as there are mounted
 *   components, all receiving the same events, all updating local
 *   state, all of them going stale in slightly different ways.
 *   Centralising the subscription in the store means the state has
 *   exactly one writer per marker type, and components just read
 *   what they need from the store.
 *
 *   The listener is installed at module load. By the time a
 *   renderer's bundle imports this module (which happens when the
 *   app mounts the store for the first time), the preload has
 *   already run and `window.electronAPI` is available. The
 *   `typeof window !== 'undefined'` guard is defensive — it costs
 *   nothing and it keeps the module from throwing if it is ever
 *   imported in a non-renderer context (a future test harness that
 *   exercises the store's reducers directly).
 *
 * ── Why the store is NOT persisted ────────────────────────────
 *
 *   Unlike `userStore` (which persists the session token) and
 *   `settingsStore` (which caches user preferences), this store
 *   holds purely transient UI state. The candidate list is
 *   regenerated by a fresh search; the progress fields are only
 *   meaningful while an operation is in flight; the error is
 *   cleared on the next attempt. Persisting any of it would
 *   produce confusing resurrections after a restart — a candidate
 *   list from an old search appearing under a topic the user is
 *   visiting for the first time in days. The store therefore uses
 *   plain `create()` with no `persist` middleware.
 *
 *   The `candidatesByTopic` and `failedByTopic` records are
 *   repopulated on first open of each topic by the
 *   `loadCandidates` action, which reads the topic's on-disk
 *   `candidates.json` and `failures.json` via the main process.
 *
 * ── The per-topic, per-bucket design ──────────────────────────
 *
 *   Every piece of research state is a record keyed by the
 *   topic's ID, so switching between topics never clobbers the
 *   previous topic's data. The marker handler routes each
 *   incoming marker to its own topic's slot using the `topicId`
 *   on the payload — a marker that arrives while the user is
 *   viewing a different topic still updates the correct slot.
 *
 *   Within a topic, the candidate list is keyed by time-range
 *   bucket (`'anytime' | 'year' | 'month' | 'week'`) rather than
 *   being a single flat array. The four buckets correspond to the
 *   four time ranges the search pipeline queries, and each bucket
 *   carries its own candidate list and its own progress slot. A
 *   bucket that was configured with a zero count is skipped
 *   entirely — it never produces candidates and never shows
 *   progress.
 *
 *   Extraction is not bucketed: it processes a flat list of URLs
 *   the user selected, and its progress is a single per-topic
 *   value. A separate `failedByTopic` record tracks URLs whose
 *   extraction failed so that the retry flow can re-run just
 *   those.
 *
 * ── Why the type-only import from listenerBridge ──────────────
 *
 *   `MarkerEvent` is the shape of every marker the store receives.
 *   It is declared in `listenerBridge.ts` — the main-process
 *   module that parses the Python scripts' `::ROOKIE::` output.
 *   The store wants that type, but it CANNOT take a runtime import
 *   of the bridge module: `listenerBridge.ts` imports
 *   `BrowserWindow` from `electron`, and `BrowserWindow` is a
 *   main-process-only export. A runtime import would crash the
 *   renderer at bundle time.
 *
 *   The fix is the `import type` form — TypeScript erases it at
 *   emit time, so the compiled renderer bundle contains no
 *   reference to `listenerBridge`. The type is available to the
 *   compiler, unavailable to the runtime, and that is exactly what
 *   is wanted.
 *
 *   The channel name `'research:marker'` is declared as a local
 *   constant in this file rather than imported from the bridge.
 *   The two strings must match; they are documented in both files
 *   as a cross-module contract. A future session that adds a
 *   runtime-safe constants module (one that both processes can
 *   import) would replace the local constant with an import; that
 *   change is not in the current plan.
 *
 * What this store does NOT do:
 *
 *   - It does not persist. See above.
 *
 *   - It does not call the backend directly. The IPC handler
 *     (`researchIPC.ts`) is the one that reaches the backend when
 *     it needs to — for example, to persist an extraction's doc
 *     metadata. The store's IPC calls go through the preload's
 *     `window.electronAPI.research.*` methods, which are the same
 *     channels every other feature's calls use.
 *
 *   - It does not own the document list. Extracted `.md` files
 *     are the documents feature's concern; the `documentStore`
 *     holds them. This store only knows the COUNT of documents
 *     the last extraction produced (`extractedCount`), which is
 *     enough for the progress panel that shows "5 docs saved".
 *
 *   - It does not handle the captcha or manual-action modals.
 *     Those markers (`CAPTCHA_REQUIRED`, `CAPTCHA_SOLVED`,
 *     `MANUAL_ACTION`) reach the renderer over the same channel
 *     and are routed by the automation store (Session 20). This
 *     store's handler explicitly ignores those marker types.
 *
 *   - It does not distinguish a partial success from a failure.
 *     A search that collected ten candidates before hitting a
 *     rate-limit is a "success" from this store's perspective —
 *     the ten candidates are in the list, the user can act on
 *     them. A future session that wants richer classification
 *     would add fields to the state; the current shape matches
 *     what the UI needs.
 */

import { create } from 'zustand';

import type { MarkerEvent } from '../services/listenerBridge.js';

// =============================================================================
//  Channel name
// =============================================================================
//
//  The IPC channel the main process emits every marker on. This
//  string MUST match `listenerBridge.ts`'s exported
//  `MARKER_EVENT_CHANNEL` constant. Declared locally because the
//  bridge module cannot be imported at runtime — see the file
//  header.

const MARKER_CHANNEL = 'research:marker';

// =============================================================================
//  Public types
// =============================================================================

/**
 * One candidate source page as the renderer receives it.
 *
 * The shape is identical to the `SearchCandidate` interface
 * declared in `searchService.ts`, but it is re-declared here
 * rather than imported because the main-process service module
 * cannot be imported from the renderer — it depends on the
 * Electron main process's `app` and on Node's child-process
 * machinery, neither of which the renderer can access.
 *
 * The two declarations must stay in sync. A marker whose candidate
 * payload is missing a field will be silently narrowed by
 * `narrowCandidate` below (dropping the malformed candidate); if
 * the two sides drift on the field SET, that narrowing will drop
 * every candidate rather than crash, which is the correct failure
 * mode — the user sees an empty list and can retry.
 */
export interface SearchCandidate {
  /** The absolute URL of the page. */
  url: string;

  /** The page's title, as displayed on the SERP. */
  title: string;

  /** The SERP snippet — a short preview of the page's content.
   *  May be an empty string when the SERP did not carry one. */
  snippet: string;

  /** The platform slug for the URL's domain — `'reddit'`,
   *  `'quora'`, `'medium'`, `'twitter'`, or `'generic'`. */
  platform: string;
}



/**
 * The four time-range buckets the search pipeline queries.
 *
 * The values match the strings the Python search scripts accept
 * on their `--time-range` argument (see `research/google_search.py`
 * and the pipeline's main.py dispatcher). They are the store's
 * canonical bucket identifiers.
 */
export type BucketKey = 'anytime' | 'year' | 'month' | 'week';

/**
 * A topic's candidate list, split by time-range bucket.
 *
 * Every bucket key is required and every value is an array — an
 * empty array is the correct representation for "searched but
 * found nothing" or "not yet searched". The store never uses a
 * missing key to mean "not searched" — that distinction is
 * carried by the presence or absence of the topic itself in
 * `candidatesByTopic`.
 */
export type CandidatesByBucket = Record<BucketKey, SearchCandidate[]>;

/**
 * The bucket-count configuration the New Topic modal supplies to
 * a search.
 *
 * The keys mirror the `searchBuckets` field on the `Topic` schema
 * and the `defaultBucketLayout` field on the `Preset` schema (see
 * the shared types modules on both the server and the desktop).
 * The value is the number of candidates to collect for that time
 * range; a zero means "skip this bucket entirely".
 */
export interface BucketConfig {
  anyTime: number;
  pastYear: number;
  pastMonth: number;
  pastWeek: number;
}

/**
 * One failed-extraction record, as loaded from the topic's
 * `failures.json` file.
 *
 * The record is written by the Python extraction dispatcher and
 * mirrors the payload the failure-tracking feature agreed on (see
 * the consolidated decisions document, Q2.4 and C6). The store
 * loads these records on topic open and uses them to drive the
 * retry flow.
 */
export interface FailedUrlRecord {
  /** The URL that failed to extract. */
  url: string;
  /** A short classification of the failure — e.g. `'timeout'`,
   *  `'site_returned_404'`, `'empty_content'`. */
  reason: string;
  /** How many times this URL has been attempted across runs. */
  attemptCount: number;
  /** ISO-8601 timestamp of the most recent attempt. */
  lastAttemptAt: string;
  /** The full error message from the most recent attempt, if the
   *  dispatcher captured one. Empty string when none was. */
  errorMessage: string;
}


/**
 * The lifecycle state of the research feature's current
 * operation. Mirrors the pattern used by every other store in the
 * codebase (`TopicStoreStatus`, `UserStoreState.isLoading`).
 *
 *   'idle'       — no operation is running, and the last one (if
 *                  any) succeeded. The default state.
 *   'searching'  — a search is in flight.
 *   'extracting' — an extraction is in flight.
 *   'error'      — the last operation failed. `error` carries a
 *                  user-facing message.
 */
export type ResearchStoreStatus =
  | 'idle'
  | 'searching'
  | 'extracting'
  | 'error';

/**
 * Live search progress, as reported by the Python script's
 * `SEARCH_PROGRESS` marker.
 *
 *   page      — the 1-based index of the page currently being
 *               processed.
 *   found     — how many candidates have been collected so far.
 *   requested — the target number the caller asked for.
 *
 * The UI uses `found / requested` as a rough progress estimate —
 * not exact, because the search may stop early (fewer results
 * than requested) or overshoot (a page returns more than the
 * remaining count).
 */
export interface SearchProgress {
  page: number;
  found: number;
  requested: number;
}

/**
 * Live extraction progress, as reported by the Python script's
 * `EXTRACT_PROGRESS` marker.
 *
 *   index  — the 1-based index of the URL currently being
 *            extracted.
 *   total  — how many URLs the caller asked to extract.
 *   url    — the URL currently being processed.
 *   status — one of `'loading'`, `'saved'`, `'failed'`. The UI
 *            renders a per-URL badge from this value.
 */
export interface ExtractProgress {
  index: number;
  total: number;
  url: string;
  status: 'loading' | 'saved' | 'failed';
}

/**
 * The full shape of the research store's state, including the
 * data fields and the actions.
 */
export interface ResearchStoreState {
  // ---- Data ----

  /** The topic that most recently started a search, or null
   *  before any search. A tracking field only — the marker
   *  handler routes each marker to its own topic's slot using
   *  the payload's `topicId` field, not this value. */
  activeTopicId: string | null;

  /** Per-topic candidate lists, split by time-range bucket.
   *  Absence of a topic's key means "not yet loaded"; the value
   *  itself always carries all four bucket keys. */
  candidatesByTopic: Record<string, CandidatesByBucket>;

  /** Per-topic per-bucket live search progress. Each topic's
   *  value is a full four-key record; a bucket's slot is null
   *  when that bucket is not currently searching. */
  searchProgressByTopic: Record<
    string,
    Record<BucketKey, SearchProgress | null>
  >;

  /** Per-topic live extraction progress. Null when the topic
   *  has no extraction in flight. */
  extractProgressByTopic: Record<string, ExtractProgress | null>;

  /** Per-topic count of documents saved by the last extraction. */
  extractedCountByTopic: Record<string, number>;

  /** Per-topic list of URLs whose extraction failed. Populated
   *  from `failures.json` on first open of the topic. */
  failedByTopic: Record<string, FailedUrlRecord[]>;

  /** Per-topic async lifecycle state. Absence of a key is
   *  treated as `'idle'`. */
  statusByTopic: Record<string, ResearchStoreStatus>;

  /** Per-topic last error message, or null. */
  errorByTopic: Record<string, string | null>;

  // ---- Actions ----

  /**
   * Run a full bucketed search for a topic. Invokes
   * `window.electronAPI.research.search(topicId, title, keywords,
   * bucketKey)` once per configured bucket, sequentially, and
   * updates per-bucket progress as each call resolves. Buckets
   * whose configured count is zero are skipped entirely.
   *
   * The candidate data itself arrives over the marker channel —
   * each SEARCH_RESULT marker carries a `timeRange` field that
   * the store routes to the corresponding bucket. The action's
   * return value reflects only whether every configured bucket's
   * IPC call returned a success envelope.
   *
   * Returns `true` when every configured bucket's search call
   * succeeded, `false` on any failure.
   */
  runFullSearch: (
    topicId: string,
    title: string,
    keywords: string[],
    bucketConfig: BucketConfig
  ) => Promise<boolean>;

  /**
   * Run an extraction on the given URLs. Invokes
   * `window.electronAPI.research.extract` and updates this
   * topic's extraction state as the operation progresses.
   *
   * Returns the number of documents saved, or 0 on failure.
   */
  runExtraction: (topicId: string, urls: string[]) => Promise<number>;

  /**
   * Re-run extraction for the URLs whose last attempt failed.
   * Reads the topic's failed-URL list, invokes the same
   * extraction flow with only those URLs, and refreshes the
   * failed list from disk afterwards (some URLs may have
   * succeeded on the retry).
   *
   * Returns the number of documents saved by the retry.
   */
  retryFailed: (topicId: string) => Promise<number>;

  /**
   * Cancel whatever research operation is running for a topic.
   * Invokes `window.electronAPI.research.cancel`, which hard-kills
   * the Python subprocess.
   */
  cancelOperation: (topicId: string) => Promise<void>;

  /**
   * Load a topic's saved candidates and failed-URL records from
   * the main process on first open. Subsequent calls for a topic
   * that already has a `candidatesByTopic` slot are no-ops.
   */
  loadCandidates: (topicId: string) => Promise<void>;

  /** Empty the candidate buckets for one topic. */
  clearCandidates: (topicId: string) => void;

  /** Clear the last error message for one topic. */
  clearError: (topicId: string) => void;

  /** Reset every field to its initial value. */
  reset: () => void;

  // ---- Per-topic getters ----

  /** All candidates for a topic, flattened across buckets. */
  getCandidates: (topicId: string) => SearchCandidate[];

  /** The full bucket-keyed candidate record for a topic. */
  getCandidatesByBucket: (topicId: string) => CandidatesByBucket;

  /** The per-bucket progress record for a topic. */
  getSearchProgress: (
    topicId: string
  ) => Record<BucketKey, SearchProgress | null>;

  /** The extraction progress for a topic, or null when none. */
  getExtractProgress: (topicId: string) => ExtractProgress | null;

  /** How many documents the last extraction saved for a topic. */
  getExtractedCount: (topicId: string) => number;

  /** The list of failed URLs for a topic. */
  getFailed: (topicId: string) => FailedUrlRecord[];

  /** The async lifecycle state for a topic. */
  getStatus: (topicId: string) => ResearchStoreStatus;

  /** The last error message for a topic, or null. */
  getError: (topicId: string) => string | null;

  /**
   * Internal — route one marker event to the correct state
   * update. Called by the module-level listener installed at
   * import time; not intended for direct use by components.
   */
  _handleMarker: (event: MarkerEvent) => void;
}

// =============================================================================
//  Initial state
// =============================================================================

/**
 * The store's initial data fields. Extracted as a constant so
 * `reset` can spread it into a new state object without
 * duplicating the literals.
 *
 * `status` starts as `'idle'`, not `'searching'` — the store does
 * not know what the user is about to do, and a `'searching'` that
 * is immediately overwritten on the next render is a needless
 * intermediate state. The active flag flips only when an action
 * actually starts.
 */
const INITIAL_STATE = {
  activeTopicId: null as string | null,
  candidatesByTopic: {} as Record<string, CandidatesByBucket>,
  searchProgressByTopic: {} as Record<
    string,
    Record<BucketKey, SearchProgress | null>
  >,
  extractProgressByTopic: {} as Record<string, ExtractProgress | null>,
  extractedCountByTopic: {} as Record<string, number>,
  failedByTopic: {} as Record<string, FailedUrlRecord[]>,
  statusByTopic: {} as Record<string, ResearchStoreStatus>,
  errorByTopic: {} as Record<string, string | null>,
};


// =============================================================================
//  Bucket helpers
// =============================================================================
//
//  Small factories and predicates shared by the initialisation
//  paths and the marker handler. Both empty-shape factories
//  return fresh objects on every call so that two topics' slots
//  never share a reference — a future mutation of one topic's
//  record cannot silently affect another's.

/**
 * The set of bucket keys as a runtime array. Its type is
 * `readonly BucketKey[]`, which guarantees at compile time that
 * every string is a legal member of the union — a typo would be
 * a type error, not a silent runtime surprise.
 */
const BUCKET_KEYS: readonly BucketKey[] = [
  'anytime',
  'year',
  'month',
  'week',
];

/**
 * Type guard: is the given value one of the four bucket keys?
 */
const isBucketKey = (value: unknown): value is BucketKey =>
  typeof value === 'string' &&
  (BUCKET_KEYS as readonly string[]).includes(value);

/**
 * Return a fresh `CandidatesByBucket` with every bucket set to an
 * empty array. Used when a topic's slot is first created.
 */
const emptyBuckets = (): CandidatesByBucket => ({
  anytime: [],
  year: [],
  month: [],
  week: [],
});

/**
 * Return a fresh per-bucket progress record with every bucket set
 * to null. Used when a topic's search progress is first created.
 */
const emptyProgressBuckets = (): Record<
  BucketKey,
  SearchProgress | null
> => ({
  anytime: null,
  year: null,
  month: null,
  week: null,
});

/**
 * Read the `timeRange` field from a marker's payload, or return
 * null. Used by the marker handler to route SEARCH_PROGRESS and
 * SEARCH_RESULT markers to their correct bucket.
 */
const readTimeRange = (payload: unknown): BucketKey | null => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) {
    return null;
  }
  const t = (payload as { timeRange?: unknown }).timeRange;
  return isBucketKey(t) ? t : null;
};


// =============================================================================
//  Marker payload narrowing
// =============================================================================
//
//  Every marker's payload arrives as `unknown` — the bridge does
//  not validate payload shapes, and neither does the store until
//  it needs to read a specific field. The narrowing helpers below
//  are the boundary where the opaque `unknown` becomes a typed
//  value the store can act on. A malformed payload is silently
//  ignored (the marker is dropped) rather than throwing — a
//  broken marker should not be able to crash the whole UI.

/**
 * Narrow a marker's payload to a shape that includes a `topicId`
 * field, or return null. Used by every marker handler to check
 * that the marker belongs to the store's currently-active topic.
 */
const readTopicId = (payload: unknown): string | null => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) {
    return null;
  }
  const t = (payload as { topicId?: unknown }).topicId;
  return typeof t === 'string' && t.length > 0 ? t : null;
};

/**
 * Narrow a `SEARCH_PROGRESS` payload into a `SearchProgress`. All
 * three numeric fields are required; a payload missing one is
 * dropped.
 */
const narrowSearchProgress = (payload: unknown): SearchProgress | null => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) return null;
  const p = payload as Record<string, unknown>;
  const page = p['page'];
  const found = p['found'];
  const requested = p['requested'];
  if (
    typeof page !== 'number' ||
    typeof found !== 'number' ||
    typeof requested !== 'number'
  ) {
    return null;
  }
  return { page, found, requested };
};

/**
 * Narrow a `SEARCH_RESULT` payload into a candidate array. Any
 * candidate entry missing `url` or `title` is dropped; the array
 * may therefore be shorter than the source, or empty.
 */
const narrowCandidates = (payload: unknown): SearchCandidate[] => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) return [];
  const raw = (payload as { candidates?: unknown }).candidates;
  if (!Array.isArray(raw)) return [];

  const result: SearchCandidate[] = [];
  for (const entry of raw) {
    if (typeof entry !== 'object' || entry === null) continue;
    const e = entry as Record<string, unknown>;
    const url = e['url'];
    const title = e['title'];
    if (typeof url !== 'string' || url.length === 0) continue;
    if (typeof title !== 'string' || title.length === 0) continue;
    const snippet = typeof e['snippet'] === 'string' ? e['snippet'] : '';
    const platform =
      typeof e['platform'] === 'string' ? e['platform'] : 'generic';
    result.push({ url, title, snippet, platform });
  }
  return result;
};

/**
 * Narrow an `EXTRACT_PROGRESS` payload into an `ExtractProgress`.
 * The `status` field is additionally constrained to the three
 * values the UI knows how to render; an unknown status is
 * normalized to `'loading'` (the safe default — it does not
 * claim a save that did not happen).
 */
const narrowExtractProgress = (
  payload: unknown
): ExtractProgress | null => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) return null;
  const p = payload as Record<string, unknown>;
  const index = p['index'];
  const total = p['total'];
  const url = p['url'];
  const statusRaw = p['status'];
  if (
    typeof index !== 'number' ||
    typeof total !== 'number' ||
    typeof url !== 'string' ||
    url.length === 0
  ) {
    return null;
  }
  const status: 'loading' | 'saved' | 'failed' =
    statusRaw === 'saved' || statusRaw === 'failed'
      ? statusRaw
      : 'loading';
  return { index, total, url, status };
};

/**
 * Narrow an `EXTRACT_DONE` payload into the number of docs saved.
 * Returns 0 for a malformed payload; a "0 saved" is a legitimate
 * value (an extraction that succeeded on the pipeline's side but
 * found nothing to save), so the fallback is not a lie.
 */
const narrowSavedCount = (payload: unknown): number => {
  if (typeof payload !== 'object' || payload === null) return 0;
  const n = (payload as { savedCount?: unknown }).savedCount;
  return typeof n === 'number' && n >= 0 ? n : 0;
};

// =============================================================================
//  Store
// =============================================================================

/**
 * The research store. See the file header for its responsibilities,
 * its subscription model, and the reasoning behind its
 * non-persistence.
 *
 * Created without middleware. The store is not persisted (see the
 * file header), does not use Immer (Zustand's built-in
 * `set(fn)` form is sufficient for the shallow updates this store
 * performs), and does not need `devtools` (the codebase does not
 * use the Redux DevTools bridge anywhere else).
 */
export const useResearchStore = create<ResearchStoreState>(
  (set, get) => ({
    // ---- Initial data ----
    ...INITIAL_STATE,

    // ---- Marker handler ----
    //
    // The store's single point of contact with the marker stream.
    // Every marker is routed to its own topic's slot using the
    // `topicId` on the payload — markers that arrive while the
    // user is viewing a different topic still update the correct
    // slot. Search-stage markers additionally carry a `timeRange`
    // field that identifies which bucket the marker's data
    // belongs to.
    _handleMarker: (event: MarkerEvent): void => {
      const topicId = readTopicId(event.payload);
      if (topicId === null) return;

      switch (event.type) {
        case 'SEARCH_PROGRESS': {
          const progress = narrowSearchProgress(event.payload);
          if (progress === null) return;
          // A SEARCH_PROGRESS marker from an older pipeline (before
          // the bucket change) would lack a `timeRange` field.
          // Route those to the `anytime` bucket so the UI still
          // shows something rather than dropping the marker
          // silently. Once the pipeline is fully upgraded every
          // marker carries a bucket and this fallback becomes
          // unreachable.
          const bucket: BucketKey =
            readTimeRange(event.payload) ?? 'anytime';
          set((state) => {
            const topicProgress =
              state.searchProgressByTopic[topicId] ??
              emptyProgressBuckets();
            return {
              searchProgressByTopic: {
                ...state.searchProgressByTopic,
                [topicId]: {
                  ...topicProgress,
                  [bucket]: progress,
                },
              },
            };
          });
          return;
        }

        case 'SEARCH_RESULT': {
          const candidates = narrowCandidates(event.payload);
          const bucket: BucketKey =
            readTimeRange(event.payload) ?? 'anytime';
          set((state) => {
            const topicBuckets =
              state.candidatesByTopic[topicId] ?? emptyBuckets();
            const topicProgress =
              state.searchProgressByTopic[topicId] ??
              emptyProgressBuckets();
            return {
              candidatesByTopic: {
                ...state.candidatesByTopic,
                [topicId]: {
                  ...topicBuckets,
                  [bucket]: candidates,
                },
              },
              // Clear this bucket's progress — the bucket is done.
              searchProgressByTopic: {
                ...state.searchProgressByTopic,
                [topicId]: {
                  ...topicProgress,
                  [bucket]: null,
                },
              },
            };
          });
          // The overall status is owned by the `runFullSearch`
          // action, not by individual SEARCH_RESULT markers — a
          // four-bucket search is not idle until every bucket's
          // IPC call has resolved.
          return;
        }

        case 'EXTRACT_PROGRESS': {
          const progress = narrowExtractProgress(event.payload);
          if (progress !== null) {
            set((state) => ({
              extractProgressByTopic: {
                ...state.extractProgressByTopic,
                [topicId]: progress,
              },
            }));
          }
          return;
        }

        case 'EXTRACT_DONE': {
          const savedCount = narrowSavedCount(event.payload);
          set((state) => ({
            extractedCountByTopic: {
              ...state.extractedCountByTopic,
              [topicId]: savedCount,
            },
            extractProgressByTopic: {
              ...state.extractProgressByTopic,
              [topicId]: null,
            },
            statusByTopic: {
              ...state.statusByTopic,
              [topicId]: 'idle',
            },
          }));
          return;
        }

        case 'TASK_END': {
          // The main process's task-boundary marker. If the
          // task ended with a failure status, ensure the
          // topic's slot is not stuck in a transient state.
          const payload = event.payload as
            | { status?: unknown }
            | null
            | undefined;
          const status =
            payload && typeof payload.status === 'string'
              ? payload.status
              : '';
          if (status === 'failed' || status === 'aborted') {
            set((state) => ({
              statusByTopic: {
                ...state.statusByTopic,
                [topicId]: 'idle',
              },
            }));
          }
          return;
        }

        default:
          // TASK_START, CAPTCHA_REQUIRED, CAPTCHA_SOLVED,
          // MANUAL_ACTION, WRITER_*, VALIDATION_* — none of
          // these are this store's concern. Each is handled
          // by the store that owns the corresponding stage
          // (automationStore, writerStore, validationStore).
          return;
      }
    },

    // ---- runFullSearch ----
    runFullSearch: async (
      topicId: string,
      title: string,
      keywords: string[],
      bucketConfig: BucketConfig
    ): Promise<boolean> => {
      // Reset this topic's search state. The previous search's
      // candidates — from any run, on any buckets — are cleared
      // because a fresh search is a fresh population.
      set((state) => ({
        activeTopicId: topicId,
        candidatesByTopic: {
          ...state.candidatesByTopic,
          [topicId]: emptyBuckets(),
        },
        searchProgressByTopic: {
          ...state.searchProgressByTopic,
          [topicId]: emptyProgressBuckets(),
        },
        statusByTopic: {
          ...state.statusByTopic,
          [topicId]: 'searching',
        },
        errorByTopic: {
          ...state.errorByTopic,
          [topicId]: null,
        },
      }));

      // The four buckets, in run order, paired with their
      // configured counts. A bucket whose count is zero is
      // dropped from the plan entirely (see Q8.3 — zero-value
      // buckets are skipped, not searched-and-empty).
      const bucketPlan: ReadonlyArray<{
        key: BucketKey;
        count: number;
      }> = [
        { key: 'anytime', count: bucketConfig.anyTime },
        { key: 'year', count: bucketConfig.pastYear },
        { key: 'month', count: bucketConfig.pastMonth },
        { key: 'week', count: bucketConfig.pastWeek },
      ].filter((entry) => entry.count > 0);

      if (bucketPlan.length === 0) {
        // Nothing to search. Mark the operation complete and
        // return success — an empty configuration is not a
        // failure, it is a no-op.
        set((state) => ({
          statusByTopic: {
            ...state.statusByTopic,
            [topicId]: 'idle',
          },
        }));
        return true;
      }

      try {
        // Sequential — not parallel — per the plan's Q8.7 /
        // C5 decision: four sequential IPC calls preserve the
        // per-bucket progress markers the UI relies on and
        // avoid Google rate-limiting a burst of parallel
        // requests from the same session.
        for (const entry of bucketPlan) {
          const result = await window.electronAPI.research.search(
            topicId,
            title,
            keywords,
            entry.key
          );

          if (!result.success) {
            set((state) => ({
              statusByTopic: {
                ...state.statusByTopic,
                [topicId]: 'error',
              },
              errorByTopic: {
                ...state.errorByTopic,
                [topicId]: result.error ?? 'Search failed.',
              },
            }));
            return false;
          }
          // Success — the bucket's candidates arrive over the
          // marker channel (SEARCH_RESULT with `timeRange`).
          // The marker handler writes them into the slot; this
          // action does not need to touch candidates directly.
        }

        set((state) => ({
          statusByTopic: {
            ...state.statusByTopic,
            [topicId]: 'idle',
          },
        }));
        return true;
      } catch (err) {
        // A rejected IPC promise is unusual — the preload
        // does not reject on business failures — but can
        // happen if the channel is unregistered (during a
        // window between app start and IPC registration)
        // or the renderer's context has been torn down.
        set((state) => ({
          statusByTopic: {
            ...state.statusByTopic,
            [topicId]: 'error',
          },
          errorByTopic: {
            ...state.errorByTopic,
            [topicId]:
              err instanceof Error
                ? err.message
                : 'Unable to reach the search service.',
          },
        }));
        return false;
      }
    },

    // ---- runExtraction ----
    runExtraction: async (
      topicId: string,
      urls: string[]
    ): Promise<number> => {
      set((state) => ({
        activeTopicId: topicId,
        extractProgressByTopic: {
          ...state.extractProgressByTopic,
          [topicId]: null,
        },
        extractedCountByTopic: {
          ...state.extractedCountByTopic,
          [topicId]: 0,
        },
        errorByTopic: {
          ...state.errorByTopic,
          [topicId]: null,
        },
        statusByTopic: {
          ...state.statusByTopic,
          [topicId]: 'extracting',
        },
      }));

      try {
        const result = await window.electronAPI.research.extract(
          topicId,
          urls
        );

        if (result.success && result.data) {
          const savedCount = result.data.savedCount;
          set((state) => ({
            extractedCountByTopic: {
              ...state.extractedCountByTopic,
              [topicId]: savedCount,
            },
            statusByTopic: {
              ...state.statusByTopic,
              [topicId]: 'idle',
            },
            extractProgressByTopic: {
              ...state.extractProgressByTopic,
              [topicId]: null,
            },
          }));
          return savedCount;
        }

        set((state) => ({
          statusByTopic: {
            ...state.statusByTopic,
            [topicId]: 'error',
          },
          errorByTopic: {
            ...state.errorByTopic,
            [topicId]: result.error ?? 'Extraction failed.',
          },
          extractProgressByTopic: {
            ...state.extractProgressByTopic,
            [topicId]: null,
          },
        }));
        return 0;
      } catch (err) {
        set((state) => ({
          statusByTopic: {
            ...state.statusByTopic,
            [topicId]: 'error',
          },
          errorByTopic: {
            ...state.errorByTopic,
            [topicId]:
              err instanceof Error
                ? err.message
                : 'Unable to reach the extraction service.',
          },
          extractProgressByTopic: {
            ...state.extractProgressByTopic,
            [topicId]: null,
          },
        }));
        return 0;
      }
    },

        // ---- retryFailed ----
    retryFailed: async (topicId: string): Promise<number> => {
      // Read the topic's failed-URL list. If there is nothing
      // to retry, return zero without invoking any IPC — a
      // retry with an empty list is not an operation.
      const failed = get().failedByTopic[topicId] ?? [];
      if (failed.length === 0) {
        return 0;
      }

      // Retry only the failed URLs. `runExtraction` will reset
