      // this topic's extraction state (progress, count, error)
      // and drive the extraction on the given subset — the
      // successful documents already on disk are not touched.
      const urls = failed.map((record) => record.url);
      const savedCount = await get().runExtraction(topicId, urls);

      // Refresh the failed list from disk. Some of the retried
      // URLs may now have succeeded, and the new `failures.json`
      // reflects which — the store's cached list must match.
      //
      // A refresh failure is logged but does not affect the
      // return value: the retry itself already happened, and
      // the caller cares about how many documents were saved,
      // not whether the failure list was refreshed. A stale
      // failed list will be re-read on the next topic open.
      try {
        const result =
          await window.electronAPI.research.getFailures(topicId);
        if (result.success && result.data) {
          set((state) => ({
            failedByTopic: {
              ...state.failedByTopic,
              [topicId]: result.data ?? [],
            },
          }));
        }
      } catch (err) {
        console.error(
          '[researchStore] retryFailed refresh failed:',
          err
        );
      }

      return savedCount;
    },


    // ---- cancelOperation ----
    cancelOperation: async (topicId: string): Promise<void> => {
      try {
        await window.electronAPI.research.cancel(topicId);
      } catch (err) {
        // Cancelling is a best-effort operation. A failure
        // here means the channel was unreachable, which is
        // itself the sign that whatever was running has
        // already stopped (the main process is gone). Log
        // for a developer; do not surface to the user.
        console.error(
          '[researchStore] cancelOperation failed:',
          err
        );
      }
      // The topic's `statusByTopic` entry is flipped by the
      // marker handler when the main process emits the `killed`
      // / `TASK_END` marker for this task. This function does
      // not optimistically set `'idle'` — doing so would race
      // against a terminal marker that arrives a moment later
      // and briefly put the UI in a state the operation never
      // actually passed through.
    },

        // ---- loadCandidates ----
    loadCandidates: async (topicId: string): Promise<void> => {
      // "Fetch on first open": if the topic already has a
      // candidates slot (even if all its buckets are empty),
      // the data has already been loaded this session and
      // there is nothing to do.
      if (get().candidatesByTopic[topicId] !== undefined) {
        return;
      }

      try {
        const candidatesResult =
          await window.electronAPI.research.getCandidates(topicId);

        if (candidatesResult.success) {
          // The IPC handler returns a bucket-keyed record. A
          // success with no `data` (the file is absent, which
          // the handler normalizes to an empty result) still
          // counts as loaded — the topic simply has no search
          // history yet.
          set((state) => ({
            candidatesByTopic: {
              ...state.candidatesByTopic,
              [topicId]: candidatesResult.data ?? emptyBuckets(),
            },
          }));
        }
        // A failure is logged but does NOT write a slot — a
        // 500 from the backend or an unreadable file should not
        // be cached as "loaded, empty" because the next mount
        // should retry.

        const failuresResult =
          await window.electronAPI.research.getFailures(topicId);

        if (failuresResult.success) {
          set((state) => ({
            failedByTopic: {
              ...state.failedByTopic,
              [topicId]: failuresResult.data ?? [],
            },
          }));
        }
      } catch (err) {
        // An IPC rejection — the channel is unregistered or the
        // renderer's context has been torn down. Same policy as
        // the failure branches above: no slot is written.
        console.error(
          '[researchStore] loadCandidates failed:',
          err
        );
      }
    },


    // ---- Clear helpers ----
    clearCandidates: (topicId: string): void => {
      set((state) => ({
        candidatesByTopic: {
          ...state.candidatesByTopic,
          [topicId]: emptyBuckets(),
        },
        searchProgressByTopic: {
          ...state.searchProgressByTopic,
          [topicId]: emptyProgressBuckets(),
        },
      }));
    },

    clearError: (topicId: string): void => {
      set((state) => ({
        errorByTopic: {
          ...state.errorByTopic,
          [topicId]: null,
        },
      }));
      if (get().statusByTopic[topicId] === 'error') {
        set((state) => ({
          statusByTopic: {
            ...state.statusByTopic,
            [topicId]: 'idle',
          },
        }));
      }
    },

    reset: (): void => {
      set({ ...INITIAL_STATE });
    },

    
    // ---- Per-topic getters ----
    // Each getter reads one topic's value from the corresponding
    // per-topic record, with a safe default for a topic that has
    // no entry yet. The getters are convenience wrappers over
    // the records, not a second source of truth.

    getCandidates: (topicId: string): SearchCandidate[] => {
      const buckets = get().candidatesByTopic[topicId];
      if (buckets === undefined) {
        return [];
      }
      // Flatten in bucket order so the output is deterministic.
      return [
        ...buckets.anytime,
        ...buckets.year,
        ...buckets.month,
        ...buckets.week,
      ];
    },

    getCandidatesByBucket: (topicId: string): CandidatesByBucket => {
      return get().candidatesByTopic[topicId] ?? emptyBuckets();
    },

    getSearchProgress: (
      topicId: string
    ): Record<BucketKey, SearchProgress | null> => {
      return (
        get().searchProgressByTopic[topicId] ?? emptyProgressBuckets()
      );
    },

    getExtractProgress: (topicId: string): ExtractProgress | null => {
      return get().extractProgressByTopic[topicId] ?? null;
    },

    getExtractedCount: (topicId: string): number => {
      return get().extractedCountByTopic[topicId] ?? 0;
    },

    getFailed: (topicId: string): FailedUrlRecord[] => {
      return get().failedByTopic[topicId] ?? [];
    },

    getStatus: (topicId: string): ResearchStoreStatus => {
      return get().statusByTopic[topicId] ?? 'idle';
    },

    getError: (topicId: string): string | null => {
      return get().errorByTopic[topicId] ?? null;
    },
  })
);

// =============================================================================
//  Marker listener installation
// =============================================================================
//
//  The listener is installed ONCE at module load. See the file
//  header for why the subscription lives at the store level rather
//  than per-component.
//
//  The guard `typeof window !== 'undefined'` protects against a
//  non-renderer context (a future test harness that imports the
//  store to exercise its reducers). When the guard fails, the
//  store still works as a pure state container — actions write
//  state, components read state — but the marker stream never
//  arrives. In the app, the guard always passes.

if (
  typeof window !== 'undefined' &&
  typeof window.electronAPI === 'object' &&
  window.electronAPI !== null
) {
  window.electronAPI.on(MARKER_CHANNEL, (raw: unknown) => {
    // The preload passes the marker through unchanged. Its
    // runtime shape is `{ type: string, payload: unknown }`.
    // The type assertion here is safe because the channel is
    // owned by the main process's listenerBridge, which emits
    // exactly that shape for every message.
    useResearchStore
      .getState()
      ._handleMarker(raw as MarkerEvent);
  });
}

// =============================================================================
//  Default export
// =============================================================================

/**
 * The hook as the default export, matching the convention used
 * by every other store in the codebase. Consumers that prefer
 * the named form use `import { useResearchStore } from ...`;
 * consumers that prefer the default form use
 * `import useResearchStore from ...`. Both resolve to the same
 * hook.
 */
export default useResearchStore;
</file>

<file path="apps/desktop/src/features/topics/pages/TopicDetail.tsx">
/**
 * WriteSpeare — topic detail page.
 *
 * The pipeline view for a single topic. A user arrives here by
 * clicking a card on the Topics home page, or by navigating
 * directly to `/dashboard/topics/:id`. From here they drive the
 * topic through every stage of the pipeline — search, extraction,
 * writing, validation — and view or export the finished article.
 *
 * The layout is a tabbed pipeline. Four tabs, one per stage the
 * user interacts with:
 *
 *   Sources    — the search candidates found for this topic. The
 *                user reviews them, unchecks any they don't want,
 *                and clicks "Fetch Selected" to move to the
 *                extraction stage. The tab hosts <SearchProgress />
 *                (the live search panel and the "Start Search"
 *                action) and <SourceList /> (the candidate rows
 *                with checkboxes).
 *
 *   Documents  — the extracted `.md` files. One row per file, with
 *                word count, platform, and a preview button. The
 *                tab hosts <DocumentList />, which owns the
 *                preview modal internally.
 *
 *   Article    — the writer output. While the writer is running,
 *                this tab shows <WriterProgress />. When an
 *                article exists, it also shows <ArticlePreview />
 *                — a Markdown render with an Edit button, a
 *                Regenerate button, and an export dropdown in its
 *                header. Both components are stacked so the user
 *                can watch progress and read the current draft
 *                without changing tabs.
 *
 *   Report     — the validation report. Six panels — readability,
 *                SEO, keywords, originality, AI markers, grammar.
 *                The tab hosts <ValidationReport />, which owns
 *                the "Run Validation" action and the six-panel
 *                layout.
 *
 * Why tabs and not a wizard:
 *
 *   A wizard — a one-way flow with Back/Next buttons — would
 *   force the user through the pipeline in order every time. But
 *   the real usage pattern is iterative: a user reviews the
 *   article, notices a problem in the report, wants to add a
 *   source, runs extraction again, and re-checks. Tabs let them
 *   jump to any stage without losing their place or having to
 *   click Back several times to reach an earlier step. The user
 *   journey document explicitly calls this out in Stage 8 —
 *   "Any topic can be re-run at any stage" — and a tabbed layout
 *   is the direct expression of that.
 *
 * Tab state — local, and derived from status until the user
 * intervenes:
 *
 *   The active tab is a piece of purely ephemeral UI state. It is
 *   NOT stored in the topic store, and it is NOT persisted. Two
 *   behaviours fall out of that decision, both deliberate:
 *
 *     1. On the first render after the topic becomes available,
 *        the tab is DERIVED from the topic's pipeline status —
 *        see `getDefaultTab` below. A topic that is
 *        `researching` opens on Sources; one that is
 *        `completed` opens on Article. The user always lands on
 *        the stage that is most likely to be useful, without
 *        having to click anything.
 *
 *     2. Once the user clicks a tab, `activeTab` becomes a
 *        concrete value and stops following the status. The user
 *        is now driving; a background status update does not
 *        yank them to a different tab mid-read.
 *
 *   The distinction between "null" (never clicked — follow the
 *   status) and a concrete tab id (user has chosen) is
 *   represented by `activeTab: TabId | null`. When it is null,
 *   `getDefaultTab(topic.status)` supplies the displayed tab.
 *   When it is set, the store's `activeTab` wins.
 *
 *   Navigating away from this topic and back resets `activeTab`
 *   to null, because the component unmounts and remounts. That
 *   is correct — the user's tab choice was scoped to the visit,
 *   not to the topic.
 *
 * Data flow — the two navigation paths:
 *
 *   The page reads `activeTopic` from the topic store. Two paths
 *   lead here:
 *
 *     1. From the Topics list — the card's click handler calls
 *        `setActiveTopic(topic)` before navigating, so the store
 *        already holds the topic by the time this component
 *        mounts. The mount effect sees the store's `activeTopic._id`
 *        matching the route param `:id` and short-circuits; no
 *        network call.
 *
 *     2. Direct navigation (a deep link, a browser refresh, a
 *        bookmark) — the store is empty. The mount effect sees
 *        the mismatch and calls `loadTopic(id)`. While the fetch
 *        is in flight, the page renders a spinner; on success,
 *        the topic renders; on failure, the error branch shows.
 *
 *   The effect is deliberately conditional — it only fires when
 *   the store's topic does not already match the route param.
 *   Unconditionally calling `loadTopic(id)` on every mount would
 *   produce a redundant network round trip on every navigation
 *   from the Topics list, which is the common case.
 * 
  *
 * Load on open — the per-topic state that follows the topic:
 *
 *   When the topic becomes available, the page also loads the
 *   per-topic state that other features cache independently:
 *   the writer's latest article for this topic, and the
 *   research feature's saved search candidates. The two loads
 *   are triggered by a dedicated effect in the component body,
 *   keyed on the same route-param value the `loadTopic` effect
 *   uses for its guard.
 *
 *   The loads are best-effort and self-guarding: the writer
 *   hook's `loadLatest` and the research hook's `loadCandidates`
 *   both skip their fetch when the topic's per-topic slot is
 *   already populated. The effect therefore fires on every
 *   mount and every route-param change, and the guards inside
 *   the actions handle the "already loaded" case cheaply —
 *   a returning visit to a topic renders with the article and
 *   the candidates already in place, rather than showing empty
 *   states until the user takes an action.
 *
 *   This is the "fetch on first open" behaviour from Q4.2. The
 *   corresponding per-topic store shape — `articlesByTopic`
 *   and `candidatesByTopic` — is what makes the load keyed by
 *   topic ID rather than scoped to a single "active" slot.
 *
 * Four render branches:
 *
 *   The page has four terminal states, checked in order:
 *
 *     1. LOADING — no matching topic in the store AND the store's
 *        status is 'loading'. A centered spinner.
 *
 *     2. ERROR — the store's status is 'error' and we still do
 *        not have a matching topic. A red banner with the error
 *        message and a "Back to topics" button.
 *
 *     3. NOT FOUND — the store has settled (status 'idle') but
 *        its `activeTopic` is null or holds a different topic's
 *        id. A neutral panel with "Topic not found" and a "Back
 *        to topics" button.
 *
 *     4. LOADED — the store's `activeTopic._id` matches the route
 *        param. The header, tabs, and tab content render.
 *
 *   The order matters: the loading check must be first (a store
 *   that is loading but has no topic must not fall through to
 *   "not found"); the error check must be second (a store that
 *   failed must not fall through to "not found" and hide the
 *   reason).
 *
 * Forward references — this file is the integration point of the
 * topics feature:
 *
 *   The page is the app's gateway to the research, documents,
 *   writer, and validation features. Every one of those features
 *   contributes a component that renders inside a tab here, and
 *   every one of those components is created in a later session
 *   of the plan:
 *
 *     SearchProgress    — Session 10, step 79
 *     SourceList        — Session 10, step 80
 *     DocumentList      — Session 12, step 96
 *     WriterProgress    — Session 14, step 108
 *     ArticlePreview    — Session 14, step 110
 *     ValidationReport  — Session 16, step 124
 *
 *   In addition to the six stage components, the page also
 *   forward-references one component from the export feature:
 *
 *     DownloadBundleModal — PR-5, "Download bundle"
 *
 *   The DownloadBundleModal is mounted at the page level (as a
 *   sibling of the header and the main element) and its import
 *   is the seventh forward reference this file carries.
 *
 *   Until those files exist, the imports below produce seven
 *   `TS2307` errors. This is the same forward-reference shape
 *   that Sessions 4, 5, and 6 have already established — see
 *   `App.tsx`, `Layout.tsx`, `Sidebar.tsx`, and the various
 *   feature pages — and it is the correct pattern. The
 *   alternative, stubbing the components with placeholders so
 *   the file compiles today, would violate the execution
 *   protocol's "no placeholder files" rule and would have to be
 *   replaced anyway when the real components land.
 *
 *   Because this file is created BEFORE the stage components
 *   (Session 7 vs. Sessions 10, 12, 14, 16), the prop interface
 *   is owned here: each stage component is expected to accept a
 *   `topic: Topic` prop when it is created. If a future session
 *   discovers that a stage component needs an additional prop —
 *   say, an `onNavigate` callback or a `readonly` flag — the
 *   change lands in this file at the same time as the stage
 *   component, keeping the interface in sync.
 *
 *  *
 * Download bundle:
 *
 *   The page header carries a "Download bundle" button that
 *   opens the `DownloadBundleModal` — the topic-level export
 *   affordance introduced by PR-5. This is distinct from the
 *   article-level `ExportMenu` that `ArticlePreview` renders
 *   (see "What this page does NOT do" below): the bundle
 *   covers the topic's entire output — article, sources,
 *   report, prompt used, preset used, and metadata — while
 *   the `ExportMenu` exports the article alone in one of the
 *   supported formats.
 *
 *   The modal is mounted at the page level, as a sibling of
 *   the header and the main element. This component tracks
 *   only whether the modal is currently open; the modal itself
 *   owns the checkbox state, the destination choice, and the
 *   IPC call that assembles the ZIP.
 * What this page does NOT do:
 *
 *   - It does not run the search, extraction, writer, validation,
 *     or export operations directly. Each of those actions is
 *     owned by the corresponding feature's hook (`useResearch`,
 *     `useDocuments`, `useWriter`, `useValidation`, `useExport`),
 *     and the stage components that render inside each tab call
 *     those hooks themselves. This page composes the tabs; it
 *     does not orchestrate the pipeline stages.
 *
 *   - It does not gate stages. A user can, in principle, open the
 *     Report tab before running validation — the tab shows the
 *     "no report yet" state and offers to run one. The page does
 *     not prevent that; the pipeline's real ordering constraints
 *     are enforced by the individual stage components (the
 *     writer cannot run without documents; validation cannot run
 *     without an article) and by the backend's status
 *     transitions.
 *
 *   - It does not persist the active tab. See the discussion
 *     above for why this is deliberate.
 *
 *   - It does not manage the topic's lifecycle. Deleting the
 *     topic, duplicating it, or editing its metadata are actions
 *     on the Topics list page — this page is the read-and-run
 *     view for a topic that already exists.
 *
 *   - It does not render the export menu directly. The
 *     `ExportMenu` component (Session 17) is rendered by
 *     `ArticlePreview`, not by this page — the export affordance
 *     belongs next to the article, not at the page level. The
 *     tabs the user interacts with here are the pipeline stages;
 *     export is an action on the article, and it lives with the
 *     article.
 */

import {
  useEffect,
  useState,
  type ReactNode,
} from 'react';
import { useNavigate, useParams } from 'react-router-dom';
import {
  AlertCircle,
  ArrowLeft,
  Download,
  FileSearch,
  FileText,
  Loader2,
  PenLine,
  ShieldCheck,
  type LucideIcon,
} from 'lucide-react';

import { useTopics } from '../hooks/useTopics.js';
import { useWriter } from '../../writer/hooks/useWriter.js';
import { useResearch } from '../../research/hooks/useResearch.js';

// ============================================================
//  Forward references — stage components
// ============================================================
//
//  Each of the six imports below resolves to a file that is
//  created in a later session of the plan. Until that file
//  exists, the import produces a TS2307 ("Cannot find module")
//  error. That is EXPECTED and is documented in the file header.
//
//  The imports are grouped with their owning session number in a
//  trailing comment so that a reader who sees a TS2307 on one of
//  these lines knows exactly which session resolves it.

import SearchProgress from '../../research/components/SearchProgress.js'; // Session 10, step 79
import SourceList from '../../research/components/SourceList.js'; // Session 10, step 80
import DocumentList from '../../documents/components/DocumentList.js'; // Session 12, step 96
import WriterProgress from '../../writer/components/WriterProgress.js'; // Session 14, step 108
import ArticlePreview from '../../writer/components/ArticlePreview.js'; // Session 14, step 110
import ValidationReport from '../../validation/components/ValidationReport.js'; // Session 16, step 124
import DownloadBundleModal from '../../export/components/DownloadBundleModal.js'; // PR-5

import type { Topic, TopicStatus } from '../../shared/types/index.js';

// ============================================================
//  Public types
// ============================================================

/**
 * The props accepted by `TopicDetail`. Currently empty — the page
 * takes no props and derives everything from the route param and
 * the topic store.
 *
 * The interface is declared anyway so that a future addition (for
 * example, an `initialTab` prop for a programmatic deep link that
 * wants to open a specific stage) has a named, exported home.
 */
// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-empty-object-type
export interface TopicDetailProps {
  // Reserved for future additions. See file header.
}

// ============================================================
//  Tab definitions
// ============================================================

/**
 * The four stage identifiers. A string-literal union rather than
 * an enum — the values are used as React keys and (potentially)
 * as part of a future URL query string for tab persistence, and
 * a plain string is the natural representation for both.
 */
type TabId = 'sources' | 'documents' | 'article' | 'report';

/**
 * The shape of one tab's static metadata. The `icon` field is
 * typed as `LucideIcon` — the same type the codebase's other
 * icon-bearing tables use — so that swapping an icon is a change
 * to this one table, not a change to the render code.
 */
interface TabDefinition {
  readonly id: TabId;
  readonly label: string;
  readonly icon: LucideIcon;
}

/**
 * The tab table, in display order. Declared as a
 * `readonly TabDefinition[]` so the array is not mutated by any
 * consumer and the compiler enforces the field types.
 *
 * The order matches the pipeline's logical progression —
 * Sources, Documents, Article, Report — so the visual left-to-
 * right reading of the tab bar is also the pipeline's sequence.
 */
const TABS: readonly TabDefinition[] = [
  { id: 'sources', label: 'Sources', icon: FileSearch },
  { id: 'documents', label: 'Documents', icon: FileText },
  { id: 'article', label: 'Article', icon: PenLine },
  { id: 'report', label: 'Report', icon: ShieldCheck },
];

// ============================================================
//  Status → default tab
// ============================================================

/**
 * Map a topic's pipeline status to the tab that is most likely
 * to be useful when the user first lands on this page.
 *
 * The mapping is grounded in the pipeline's own semantics:
 *
 *   - `draft`        — no work has started. Sources is where the
 *                      user clicks "Start Search", so it is the
 *                      natural landing point.
 *   - `researching`  — search is in flight. Sources shows the
 *                      live progress.
 *   - `extracting`   — extraction is in flight. Sources still
 *                      owns the progress panel (the extraction
 *                      panel lives inside the same stage flow).
 *   - `extracted`    — documents are ready. Documents is where
 *                      the user reviews what was fetched.
 *   - `writing`      — the writer is running. Article shows the
 *                      live progress.
 *   - `validating`   — validation is running. Report shows the
 *                      live progress.
 *   - `completed`    — everything is done. Article is the
 *                      finished output the user wants to read.
 *   - `failed`       — something broke. Sources is the safest
 *                      default; the user can inspect earlier
 *                      stages to diagnose.
 *
 * The function is total over `TopicStatus` — every member of the
 * union is handled by a case. If a future session adds a status
 * to the union, TypeScript's exhaustiveness checking will report
 * a "not all code paths return a value" error here, which is the
 * correct prompt to extend the switch.
 */
const getDefaultTab = (status: TopicStatus): TabId => {
  switch (status) {
    case 'researching':
    case 'extracting':
      return 'sources';
    case 'extracted':
      return 'documents';
    case 'writing':
    case 'completed':
      return 'article';
    case 'validating':
      return 'report';
    case 'draft':
    case 'failed':
      return 'sources';
  }
};

// ============================================================
//  Status badge
// ============================================================

/**
 * Human-readable labels for each pipeline status. Rendered inside
 * the status badge in the page header so the user can see at a
 * glance where the topic is.
 *
 * `Record<TopicStatus, string>` rather than a plain object so the
 * compiler enforces exhaustiveness — adding a status to the union
 * without adding a label here is a compile error, not a runtime
 * `undefined`.
 */
const STATUS_LABELS: Record<TopicStatus, string> = {
  draft: 'Draft',
  researching: 'Researching',
  extracting: 'Extracting',
  extracted: 'Extracted',
  writing: 'Writing',
  validating: 'Validating',
  completed: 'Completed',
  failed: 'Failed',
};

/**
 * Tailwind class sets for the status badge. Same exhaustiveness
 * guarantee as `STATUS_LABELS`.
 *
 * The color mapping is semantic:
 *   - draft        → neutral (no activity yet)
 *   - researching  → sky (a read-only phase in flight)
 *   - extracting   → sky (same reason)
 *   - extracted    → sky (a checkpoint, not yet done)
 *   - writing      → amber (a write phase in flight)
 *   - validating   → amber (a write phase in flight)
 *   - completed    → emerald (done)
 *   - failed       → red (a problem)
 */
const STATUS_CLASSES: Record<TopicStatus, string> = {
  draft:
    'bg-neutral-100 text-neutral-700 ' +
    'dark:bg-neutral-800 dark:text-neutral-300',
  researching:
    'bg-sky-100 text-sky-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-sky-950 dark:text-sky-200',
  extracting:
    'bg-sky-100 text-sky-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-sky-950 dark:text-sky-200',
  extracted:
    'bg-sky-100 text-sky-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-sky-950 dark:text-sky-200',
  writing:
    'bg-amber-100 text-amber-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-amber-950 dark:text-amber-200',
  validating:
    'bg-amber-100 text-amber-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-amber-950 dark:text-amber-200',
  completed:
    'bg-emerald-100 text-emerald-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-emerald-950 dark:text-emerald-200',
  failed:
    'bg-red-100 text-red-800 ' +
    'dark:bg-red-950 dark:text-red-200',
};

// ============================================================
//  Internal: StatusBadge
// ============================================================

/**
 * The status badge rendered in the page header next to the topic
 * title. Not exported — it has no consumer outside this file.
 */
const StatusBadge = ({ status }: { status: TopicStatus }) => (
  <span
    className={
      'inline-flex items-center rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 ' +
      'text-xs font-medium ' +
      STATUS_CLASSES[status]
    }
  >
    {STATUS_LABELS[status]}
  </span>
);

// ============================================================
//  Internal: TabButton
// ============================================================

interface TabButtonProps {
  tab: TabDefinition;
  active: boolean;
  onSelect: (id: TabId) => void;
}

/**
 * One button in the tab bar. The active state is expressed both
 * visually (a `border-primary` underline) and semantically
 * (`aria-selected="true"` on the button, `role="tab"` on the
 * button's role), so an assistive technology announces the
 * current stage correctly.
 */
const TabButton = ({ tab, active, onSelect }: TabButtonProps) => {
  const Icon = tab.icon;

  const handleClick = (): void => {
    onSelect(tab.id);
  };

  return (
    <button
      type="button"
      role="tab"
      aria-selected={active}
      onClick={handleClick}
      className={
        'flex items-center gap-2 border-b-2 px-4 py-3 text-sm ' +
        'font-medium transition-colors focus-visible:outline-none ' +
        (active
          ? 'border-primary text-primary'
          : 'border-transparent text-neutral-500 ' +
            'hover:border-neutral-300 hover:text-neutral-700 ' +
            'dark:text-neutral-400 dark:hover:border-neutral-600 ' +
            'dark:hover:text-neutral-200')
      }
    >
      <Icon size={16} aria-hidden="true" />
      <span>{tab.label}</span>
    </button>
  );
};

// ============================================================
//  Internal: renderTabContent
// ============================================================

/**
 * Render the currently-selected tab's content.
 *
 * A module-level function rather than a component so the tab
 * table above and the switch below are the only two places the
 * four stage names appear — a reader who wants to know "what
 * renders inside the Documents tab?" has exactly one answer to
 * find.
 *
 * Each branch passes the FULL topic object rather than just the
 * id. The stage components need different subsets of the topic's
 * fields — the search flow needs `title` and `keywords`, the
 * writer needs `wordCount` and `promptFile`, the export flow
 * needs `title` for the default filename — and passing the whole
 * object keeps the interface uniform across all six components.
 * See the file header for the rationale on why this file owns
 * that interface (it is created before the stage components).
 */
const renderTabContent = (topic: Topic, tab: TabId): ReactNode => {
  switch (tab) {
    case 'sources':
      return (
        <div className="space-y-6">
          <SearchProgress topic={topic} />
          <SourceList topic={topic} />
        </div>
      );
    case 'documents':
      return <DocumentList topic={topic} />;
    case 'article':
      return (
        <div className="space-y-6">
          <WriterProgress topic={topic} />
          <ArticlePreview topic={topic} />
        </div>
      );
    case 'report':
      return <ValidationReport topic={topic} />;
  }
};

// ============================================================
//  TopicDetail
// ============================================================

/**
 * The topic detail page. See the file header for the page's
 * responsibilities, its four render branches, and the reasoning
 * behind the tab structure and the derived-default-tab model.
 */
export const TopicDetail = (_props: TopicDetailProps = {}) => {
  const params = useParams<{ id: string }>();
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  const { activeTopic, status, error, loadTopic } = useTopics();

  /**
   * The route's `:id` parameter. `useParams` types it as
   * `string | undefined` because the route pattern's `:id`
   * segment could theoretically be absent if the router matched
   * a different route — the guard below narrows the value before
   * any use.
   */
  const topicId = params['id'];

  // ---- Load-on-open hooks (PR-1, Q4.2) ----
  // Two hooks whose per-topic "first open" loading is triggered
  // by the effect further down. Both hooks take the topic's ID
  // as their required first argument (Q4.3). The `?? ''` fallback
  // is used because the route param is typed `string | undefined`
  // — the hooks must be called unconditionally, so an empty
  // string stands in when the route has no `:id` segment. In
  // that state the effect below skips its work via the
  // `if (!topicId)` guard, so the fallback value is never used
  // to fetch. Only the two `load*` actions are destructured —
  // the component reads no writer or research data.
  const { loadLatest } = useWriter(topicId ?? '');
  const { loadCandidates } = useResearch(topicId ?? '');

  /**
   * The user's tab selection, or `null` when the user has not yet
   * clicked a tab on this visit. See the file header for why the
   * distinction between `null` and a concrete value matters.
   */
  const [activeTab, setActiveTab] = useState<TabId | null>(null);

  // ---- Download-bundle modal (PR-5) ----
  // Controls the visibility of the DownloadBundleModal. The
  // modal itself owns the checkbox state, the destination
  // choice, and the IPC call; this component only tracks
  // whether the modal is currently mounted.
  const [bundleModalOpen, setBundleModalOpen] = useState(false);

  // ---- Load the topic if the store does not already hold it ----
  //
  // The effect fires on mount and on any change to the three
  // inputs. The two early-return guards implement the "don't
  // refetch what we already have" rule:
  //
  //   1. No route param — nothing to load; the render branches
  //      below handle the missing id.
  //   2. The store already holds a topic whose `_id` matches the
  //      route param — the common case when navigating from the
  //      Topics list. Nothing to do.
  //
  // The `void` on the `loadTopic` call discards the returned
  // promise; the store's `status` field is what the render reacts
  // to, and this effect has no further work to do when the
  // promise resolves.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!topicId) {
      return;
    }
    if (activeTopic !== null && activeTopic._id === topicId) {
      return;
    }
    void loadTopic(topicId);
  }, [topicId, activeTopic, loadTopic]);

  
  // ---- Load per-topic writer and research state on first open ----
  //
  // Q4.2's "fetch on first open" behaviour: when the user opens
  // a topic, the page loads the topic's latest article (writer
  // hook's `loadLatest`) and its saved search candidates
  // (research hook's `loadCandidates`). Both actions have their
  // own "already loaded" guard inside the store — they skip the
  // fetch if the topic's slot is already populated — so the
  // effect fires on every mount and on every route-param change
  // without producing redundant network round trips.
  //
  // The effect is separate from the `loadTopic` effect above
  // because the two have different dependencies and different
  // responsibilities: `loadTopic` fetches the topic's own record
  // from the backend, while this effect fetches the per-topic
  // state that the writer and research stores cache
  // independently.
  //
  // The two `load*` references are stable Zustand action
  // references — they are the same function object across every
  // render — so the dependency array's only effective trigger
  // is `topicId`. A change to the route param fires the effect
  // once; a re-render caused by unrelated state changes does
  // not re-run it.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!topicId) {
      return;
    }
    void loadLatest(topicId);
    void loadCandidates(topicId);
  }, [topicId, loadLatest, loadCandidates]);


  // ---- Back navigation ----
  //
  // The only navigation this page performs on its own is the back
  // button. Stage-specific navigations (say, "open this document's
  // preview") are owned by the stage components themselves.
  const handleBack = (): void => {
    navigate('/dashboard/topics');
  };

  // ---- Resolve the displayed tab ----
  //
  // If the user has clicked a tab this visit, use their choice.
  // Otherwise, derive the default from the loaded topic's status.
  // See the file header for the full rationale.
  //
  // The resolution happens against the LOADED topic below, since
  // the derived branch needs a topic to read the status from.
  // The three non-loaded branches do not need a tab id.

  // ---- Branch 1: loading ----
  //
  // The store is loading AND we do not have a matching topic yet.
  // The `activeTopic._id !== topicId` check is what keeps a
  // background refresh (which flips `status` to 'loading' while
  // the existing topic is still in the store) from hiding the
  // page behind a spinner.
  const isInitialLoad =
    status === 'loading' &&
    (activeTopic === null || activeTopic._id !== topicId);

  if (isInitialLoad) {
    return (
      <div className="flex h-full items-center justify-center">
        <Loader2
          size={28}
          className="animate-spin text-primary"
          aria-hidden="true"
        />
        <span className="sr-only">Loading topic…</span>
      </div>
    );
  }

  // ---- Branch 2: error ----
  //
  // The store reports an error AND we still do not have a matching
  // topic. Once a topic is loaded, a subsequent error from a
  // background refresh does not hide the page — it is up to the
  // individual stage components to surface their own errors.
  const isUnrecoverableError =
    status === 'error' &&
    (activeTopic === null || activeTopic._id !== topicId);

  if (isUnrecoverableError) {
    return (
      <div
        role="alert"
        className={
          'mx-auto mt-12 flex max-w-lg flex-col items-center gap-3 ' +
          'rounded-xl border border-red-200 bg-red-50 p-6 text-center ' +
          'dark:border-red-900 dark:bg-red-950/60'
        }
      >
        <AlertCircle
          size={24}
          className="text-red-600 dark:text-red-400"
          aria-hidden="true"
        />
        <p className="text-sm text-red-900 dark:text-red-100">
          {error ?? 'Unable to load topic.'}
        </p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={handleBack}
          className={
            'rounded-lg bg-red-600 px-4 py-2 text-sm font-medium ' +
            'text-white transition-colors hover:bg-red-700 ' +
            'focus-visible:outline-none'
          }
        >
          Back to topics
        </button>
      </div>
    );
  }

  // ---- Branch 3: not found ----
  //
  // The store has settled (status is 'idle') but `activeTopic` is
  // null or holds a different topic's id. This is the state a user
  // reaches by typing an arbitrary id into the URL — the backend
  // returned a 404 (or the initial load never ran because the
  // route param was empty), and the store has not populated
  // `activeTopic`. The message is neutral rather than an error
  // because "the topic you asked for does not exist" is a
  // legitimate answer, not a failure.
  if (activeTopic === null || activeTopic._id !== topicId) {
    return (
      <div
        className={
          'mx-auto mt-12 flex max-w-lg flex-col items-center gap-3 ' +
          'rounded-xl border border-neutral-200 bg-background p-6 ' +
          'text-center dark:border-neutral-800'
        }
      >
        <p className="text-sm text-neutral-600 dark:text-neutral-300">
          Topic not found.
        </p>
        <button
          type="button"
          onClick={handleBack}
          className={
            'rounded-lg bg-primary px-4 py-2 text-sm font-medium ' +
            'text-white transition-colors hover:bg-primary-dark ' +
            'focus-visible:outline-none'
          }
        >
          Back to topics
        </button>
      </div>
    );
  }

  // ---- Branch 4: loaded ----
  //
  // At this point `activeTopic` is non-null and matches the route
  // param. The tab is resolved here — either the user's choice
  // (`activeTab !== null`) or the status-derived default.
  const displayedTab: TabId =
    activeTab !== null
      ? activeTab
      : getDefaultTab(activeTopic.status);

  return (
    <div className="flex h-full flex-col">
      {/* ---- Header ---- */}
      {/* The header carries the back button, the topic title, and
          a compact metadata row: status badge, keyword count,
          source-count target, word-count target. Everything the
          user needs to confirm "this is the topic I meant to
          open" without scrolling. The header is fixed
          (`shrink-0`) so a long tab body scrolls beneath it. */}
      <header
        className={
          'shrink-0 border-b border-neutral-200 bg-background ' +
          'dark:border-neutral-800'
        }
      >
        <div className="mx-auto flex w-full max-w-6xl items-center gap-3 px-6 pt-4">
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={handleBack}
            aria-label="Back to topics"
            className={
              'shrink-0 rounded-md p-1 text-neutral-500 ' +
              'transition-colors hover:bg-neutral-100 ' +
              'hover:text-neutral-800 ' +
              'dark:text-neutral-400 dark:hover:bg-neutral-800 ' +
              'dark:hover:text-neutral-100 focus-visible:outline-none'
            }
          >
            <ArrowLeft size={18} aria-hidden="true" />
          </button>

          <div className="min-w-0 flex-1">
            <h1 className="truncate text-lg font-semibold text-text dark:text-neutral-100">
              {activeTopic.title}
            </h1>
            <div
              className={
                'mt-1 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 ' +
                'text-xs text-neutral-500 dark:text-neutral-400'
              }
            >
              <StatusBadge status={activeTopic.status} />
              <span aria-hidden="true">·</span>
              <span>{activeTopic.keywords.length} keywords</span>
              <span aria-hidden="true">·</span>
              <span>{activeTopic.sourceCount} sources target</span>
              <span aria-hidden="true">·</span>
              <span>{activeTopic.wordCount} words target</span>
            </div>
          </div>

          {/* ---- Download bundle action (PR-5) ---- */}
          {/* Sits on the header's right side, aligned with the
              title block. The modal this button opens is mounted
              at the bottom of the page (see the JSX below the
              <main> element). */}
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={() => setBundleModalOpen(true)}
            className={
              'shrink-0 inline-flex items-center gap-2 ' +
              'rounded-lg bg-primary px-4 py-2 text-sm ' +
              'font-medium text-white transition-colors ' +
              'hover:bg-primary-dark focus-visible:outline-none'
            }
          >
            <Download size={16} aria-hidden="true" />
            <span>Download bundle</span>
          </button>
        </div>

        {/* ---- Tab bar ---- */}
        {/* `role="tablist"` on the nav, `role="tab"` on each
            button, `aria-selected` reflecting the current tab.
            The bar sits at the bottom edge of the header so the
            active-tab underline reads as a continuation of the
            header's own bottom border. */}
        <nav
          role="tablist"
          aria-label="Pipeline stages"
          className="mx-auto flex w-full max-w-6xl gap-1 px-6"
        >
          {TABS.map((tab) => (
            <TabButton
              key={tab.id}
              tab={tab}
              active={tab.id === displayedTab}
              onSelect={setActiveTab}
            />
          ))}
        </nav>
      </header>

      {/* ---- Tab content ---- */}
      {/* `min-h-0` is required so that a child with a height
          constraint (a document preview modal, a scrollable
          report panel) can shrink below its content height inside
          the flex column. Without it, the flex layout would
          expand to fit the child's content, and `overflow-y-auto`
          below would never actually scroll. */}
      <main className="min-h-0 flex-1 overflow-y-auto">
        <div className="mx-auto w-full max-w-6xl px-6 py-6">
          {renderTabContent(activeTopic, displayedTab)}
        </div>
      </main>

      {/* ---- Download bundle modal (PR-5) ---- */}
      {/* Mounted unconditionally at the page level. The modal's
          own `open` prop controls visibility; the shared Modal
          shell it uses renders nothing when closed, so there is
          no cost to keeping it in the tree. The `topic` prop is
          the loaded topic, which the modal uses to compute the
          default filename and to locate the topic's on-disk
          folders. */}
      <DownloadBundleModal
        open={bundleModalOpen}
        onClose={() => setBundleModalOpen(false)}
        topic={activeTopic}
      />
    </div>
  );
};

// ============================================================
//  Default export
// ============================================================

/**
 * The `TopicDetail` component as the default export. The router
 * in `renderer/App.tsx` imports it with the default form
 * (`import TopicDetail from '.../TopicDetail.js'`), so this is
 * the export that keeps the route tree resolving.
 */
export default TopicDetail;
</file>

<file path="apps/desktop/src/features/shared/types/index.ts">
/**
 * WriteSpeare — shared domain types for the desktop app.
 *
 * This module is the desktop's mirror of the backend's
 * `apps/server/src/features/shared/types/index.ts`. It declares
 * every domain entity, every request payload, and every status
 * union that crosses the HTTP boundary between the two apps.
 * Both sides of the desktop app — the main process (which makes
 * the HTTP requests and parses the responses) and the renderer
 * (which renders the parsed data) — import from here.
 *
 * The two apps are independent compilation units:
 *
 *   The desktop app and the backend app share no TypeScript
 *   source. The plan's golden rules make this explicit — the
 *   boundary between them is HTTP and JSON, not an import. Both
 *   sides declare their own copy of each shared shape, and the
 *   two declarations are kept in sync by hand. When a field is
 *   added to a backend entity, the corresponding field must be
 *   added here in the same change, or the desktop will silently
 *   omit it from the objects it constructs or fails to render
 *   it when it arrives. This is the accepted cost of the
 *   two-app architecture; a shared package was considered and
 *   rejected because it would force the two apps to be built
 *   and deployed together.
 *
 * Wire format vs. backend's in-process shape:
 *
 *   Where the backend's types describe an in-process Mongoose
 *   document, this module describes what the same data looks
 *   like after the JSON round trip. Three differences are
 *   systematic:
 *
 *     1. IDs. The backend declares `_id` and every foreign key
 *        as `string | ObjectId` — the Mongoose `MongoId` type.
 *        A JSON response serializes an ObjectId to its hex
 *        string form, so on the desktop every ID is a `string`.
 *        This module uses `string` for every ID field without
 *        exception.
 *
 *     2. Timestamps. The backend declares `createdAt`,
 *        `updatedAt`, and every other date field as `Date`. A
 *        JSON response serializes a Date to its ISO-8601 string
 *        form, and axios does not parse strings into Dates. So
 *        on the desktop every timestamp is a `string`, and any
 *        component that needs a `Date` object constructs one at
 *        the display site (`new Date(createdAt)`).
 *
 *     3. The password hash. The backend's `User` interface
 *        includes `passwordHash` because the field is part of
 *        the entity. The backend strips it before serializing,
 *        so the desktop's `User` does NOT include the field at
 *        all. A consumer that tried to read `user.passwordHash`
 *        would be a compile error, which is the correct
 *        behaviour — the field never reaches the desktop, so
 *        the desktop should not be able to reference it.
 *
 *   Two types declared on the backend — `MongoId` and
 *   `PaginatedResponse<T>` — are not declared here. `MongoId`
 *   exists only to describe the union of serialized and
 *   in-process forms, and there is no in-process form on the
 *   desktop. `PaginatedResponse<T>` is not used by any
 *   endpoint the desktop consumes; the topics list, the
 *   documents list, and the reports list all return plain
 *   arrays.
 *
 * Re-export of PresetName and ProfileName:
 *
 *   The two literal-union types `PresetName` and `ProfileName`
 *   are declared in `../constants/index.ts` — where their
 *   runtime arrays live — and re-exported here so that a
 *   consumer that wants to import "everything domain-shaped"
 *   from this module can do so without a second import from
 *   the constants module. The constants module remains the
 *   single source of truth for both the arrays and the derived
 *   unions; this module's re-export is a convenience, not a
 *   second declaration.
 *
 * UserRole is a union type, not an enum:
 *
 *   The backend declares `UserRole` as a string enum
 *   (`UserRole.ADMIN = 'admin'`, `UserRole.USER = 'user'`).
 *   The enum has a runtime representation, which the backend
 *   uses in `requireAdmin` middleware to compare a caller's
 *   role against the admin value. The desktop never performs
 *   that comparison — the backend rejects a non-admin request
 *   with a 403, and the renderer's `Layout` component only
 *   reads the role to decide whether to show the "Admin" nav
 *   link. A union type (`'admin' | 'user'`) is enough for both
 *   uses and has no runtime cost. The full literal set is
 *   declared here rather than imported because the desktop's
 *   union and the backend's enum are independent declarations
 *   of the same two values.
 */

import type {
  PresetName,
  ProfileName,
} from '../constants/index.js';

// Re-export the two constants-declared unions so that a
// consumer can import "everything domain-shaped" from one
// module. See the file header for why the constants module is
// the source of truth and this re-export is a convenience.
export type { PresetName, ProfileName } from '../constants/index.js';

// ============================================================
//  API response envelope
// ============================================================

/**
 * The shape of every response that crosses the backend's HTTP
 * boundary — success or failure.
 *
 * The `success` flag is the discriminator. On `true`, `data`
 * carries the payload; on `false`, `error` carries a
 * human-readable message. `message` is reserved for optional
 * non-fatal context that a caller may surface as a toast.
 *
 * The generic parameter `T` is unconstrained; consumers
 * instantiate it with the payload type of the specific endpoint
 * they are calling. A default of `null` is used for endpoints
 * that return no payload (`logout`, `clearToken`) so that a
 * caller does not have to write `ApiResponse<null>` in every
 * case.
 */
export interface ApiResponse<T = null> {
  success: boolean;
  data?: T;
  message?: string;
  error?: string;
}

// ============================================================
//  User & Authentication
// ============================================================

/**
 * The two roles a user account can hold.
 *
 * See the file header for why this is a union type rather than
 * an enum.
 */
export type UserRole = 'admin' | 'user';

/**
 * The public shape of a user account as the desktop receives it
 * from the backend.
 *
 * The `passwordHash` field that the backend's `User` interface
 * declares is deliberately absent — the backend strips it before
 * serializing, so the field never reaches the desktop. See the
 * file header for the systematic ID and timestamp conversions
 * that apply to every entity in this module.
 */
export interface User {
  _id: string;
  email: string;
  role: UserRole;
  active: boolean;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

/**
 * The payload the admin feature sends when creating a new user
 * through the `POST /api/users` endpoint.
 */
export interface UserCreatePayload {
  email: string;
  password: string;
  role?: UserRole;
  active?: boolean;
}

/**
 * The payload for a partial update to a user. Every field is
 * optional; the backend merges the patch onto the existing
 * document.
 */
export interface UserUpdatePayload {
  email?: string;
  password?: string;
  role?: UserRole;
  active?: boolean;
}

/**
 * The body of a login request. Sent to `POST /api/auth/login`.
 */
export interface LoginRequest {
  email: string;
  password: string;
}

/**
 * The body of a successful login response.
 *
 * The `user` object is the desktop's `User` type — which is
 * already the client-safe shape, with the password hash
 * stripped. The `token` is a bearer JWT the desktop stores in
 * the encrypted `electron-store` and attaches to every
 * subsequent request through the axios client's request
 * interceptor.
 */
export interface LoginResponse {
  user: User;
  token: string;
}

// ============================================================
//  Topic
// ============================================================

/**
 * The lifecycle state of a topic. Matches the backend's
 * `TopicStatus` union exactly — the string values are what
 * crosses the wire, and the backend's schema validates the
 * value against the same set.
 */
export type TopicStatus =
  | 'draft'
  | 'researching'
  | 'extracting'
  | 'extracted'
  | 'writing'
  | 'validating'
  | 'completed'
  | 'failed';

/**
 * A topic — the atomic unit of work in WriteSpeare. One topic
 * owns one pipeline: search → extraction → writing → validation
 * → export. Every document, article, and report references its
 * owning topic by `_id`.
 */
export interface Topic {
  _id: string;
  userId: string;
  title: string;
  keywords: string[];
  preset: PresetName;
  profile: ProfileName;
  promptFile: string;
  wordCount: number;
  sourceCount: number;
  status: TopicStatus;
  /** Populated only when `status === 'failed'`. */
  error?: string;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

/**
 * The payload for creating a topic.
 *
 * `userId` is derived from the authenticated session on the
 * backend and is not part of the payload. The remaining fields
 * are the values the user chose in the New Topic modal, all
 * optional except the title.
 */
export interface TopicCreatePayload {
  title: string;
  keywords?: string[];
  preset?: PresetName;
  profile?: ProfileName;
  promptFile?: string;
  wordCount?: number;
  sourceCount?: number;
}

/**
 * The payload for a partial update to a topic. Every field is
 * optional; the backend merges the patch onto the existing
 * document.
 */
export interface TopicUpdatePayload {
  title?: string;
  keywords?: string[];
  preset?: PresetName;
  profile?: ProfileName;
  promptFile?: string;
  wordCount?: number;
  sourceCount?: number;
  status?: TopicStatus;
  error?: string;
}

/**
 * The narrowing criteria the topics list supports.
 *
 * The backend's `GET /api/topics` endpoint accepts an optional
 * `?status=` query parameter; this type models that filter. It
 * is the closest equivalent on the desktop to the ROOKIE plan's
 * `RunFilters` concept — a small, closed-set filter object
 * passed through to a list endpoint. If a future session adds
 * another filter dimension (a preset filter, a date range), it
 * is added here.
 */
export interface TopicFilters {
  status?: TopicStatus;
}

// ============================================================
//  Document
// ============================================================

/**
 * A scraped source page attached to a topic.
 *
 * The `content` field carries the extracted Markdown body of the
 * page — the same text the extraction pipeline wrote to the
 * `.md` file on disk. The desktop's document list uses the
 * other fields (title, url, platform, wordCount) for display
 * and only loads `content` when the user opens the preview.
 */
export interface Document {
  _id: string;
  topicId: string;
  userId: string;
  url: string;
  platform: string;
  title: string;
  content: string;
  wordCount: number;
  fetchedAt: string;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

// ============================================================
//  Article
// ============================================================

/**
 * The lifecycle state of a generated article. Matches the
 * backend's `ArticleStatus` union.
 */
export type ArticleStatus = 'draft' | 'validated' | 'exported';

/**
 * A generated article attached to a topic. A topic can own
 * multiple articles — one per regeneration — and the article
 * with the highest `version` number is the current one.
 */
export interface Article {
  _id: string;
  topicId: string;
  userId: string;
  content: string;
  wordCount: number;
  version: number;
  status: ArticleStatus;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

// ============================================================
//  Report
// ============================================================

/**
 * The pass/warn/fail classification of a validation section.
 * Matches the backend's `ReportStatus` union.
 */
export type ReportStatus = 'good' | 'warning' | 'fail';

/**
 * The readability section of a validation report. Values are
 * produced by `textstat` on the article body with Markdown
 * markup stripped.
 */
export interface ReadabilityReport {
  /** 0–100, higher is easier to read. Plain-English band: 60–70. */
  fleschReadingEase: number;
  /** Approximate US school grade level. Target band: 7–9. */
  fleschKincaidGrade: number;
  /** Gunning Fog index. Target: under 12. */
  gunningFog: number;
  /** SMOG index. Target: under 12. */
  smogIndex: number;
  /** Average words per sentence across the article. */
  avgSentenceLength: number;
  /** Estimated reading time for an average adult reader, in seconds. */
  readingTimeSeconds: number;
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * The structural SEO section of a report. Measures shape, not
 * content quality — heading counts, paragraph sizes, and the
 * ratio of passive to active voice.
 */
export interface SeoReport {
  wordCount: number;
  h2Count: number;
  h3Count: number;
  /** Average words per paragraph, split on blank-line boundaries. */
  avgParagraphWords: number;
  /** 0–1; fraction of sentences flagged as passive by the parser. */
  passiveVoicePercent: number;
  /** 0–1; fraction of sentences containing a transition word. */
  transitionWordsPercent: number;
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * Per-keyword placement and density. One entry per keyword the
 * user supplied on the topic, plus any keyword extracted from
 * the source corpus and accepted by the user.
 */
export interface KeywordReportEntry {
  keyword: string;
  priority: 'primary' | 'secondary' | 'supporting';
  /** Total occurrences in the article body. */
  count: number;
  /** `count / wordCount`, expressed as a fraction (0–1). */
  density: number;
  /** Whether the keyword appears in the H1 title. */
  inTitle: boolean;
  /** Whether the keyword appears in the first 100 words. */
  inIntro: boolean;
  /** Number of H2 sections whose heading contains the keyword. */
  inH2Count: number;
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * The keywords section of a report. Wraps the per-entry array
 * with an aggregate status so the section matches the shape of
 * every other section.
 */
export interface KeywordReport {
  keywords: KeywordReportEntry[];
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * One sentence from the generated article that scored above the
 * configured similarity threshold against one or more source
 * documents.
 */
export interface FlaggedSentence {
  text: string;
  /** 0–1; the highest similarity this sentence scored against any source. */
  similarity: number;
  /** Human-readable label of the source document it matched. */
  sourceTitle: string;
}

/**
 * The originality section of a report. Two independent
 * similarity signals are reported side by side: TF-IDF catches
 * lexical overlap, semantic similarity catches paraphrase.
 */
export interface OriginalityReport {
  /** Highest TF-IDF cosine similarity against any source doc. */
  maxTfidfSimilarity: number;
  /** Highest embedding cosine similarity against any source doc. */
  maxSemanticSimilarity: number;
  flaggedSentences: FlaggedSentence[];
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * One overused AI-tell phrase and how many times it appeared.
 */
export interface AiMarkerEntry {
  phrase: string;
  count: number;
}

/**
 * The AI-marker section of a report. Counts occurrences of
 * phrases that are statistically over-represented in LLM output.
 */
export interface AiMarkersReport {
  markerCount: number;
  markers: AiMarkerEntry[];
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * One grammatical or stylistic issue raised by LanguageTool.
 * `offset` and `length` are character positions in the article
 * body, used by the UI to highlight the offending span.
 */
export interface GrammarIssue {
  ruleId: string;
  message: string;
  offset: number;
  length: number;
  replacements: string[];
}

/**
 * The grammar section of a report.
 */
export interface GrammarReport {
  issueCount: number;
  issues: GrammarIssue[];
  status: ReportStatus;
}

/**
 * The complete validation report for one article. Every section
 * is required — a report is only written when all six validators
 * have completed, so a partially-populated report would indicate
 * a bug in the runner.
 */
export interface Report {
  _id: string;
  articleId: string;
  topicId: string;
  userId: string;
  readability: ReadabilityReport;
  seo: SeoReport;
  keywords: KeywordReport;
  originality: OriginalityReport;
  aiMarkers: AiMarkersReport;
  grammar: GrammarReport;
  /** The weakest status across all six sections. */
  overallStatus: ReportStatus;
  createdAt: string;
}

// ============================================================
//  Preset
// ============================================================

/**
 * A saved bundle of pipeline settings. Three system presets ship
 * with the app (Quick, Balanced, Deep) and have
 * `userId === null`; user-created presets carry the owning
 * user's id.
 *
 * A preset is self-contained — every field the pipeline reads at
 * run time is present here, so loading a preset replaces the
 * entire effective configuration rather than merging with a
 * global default. That guarantees the same preset produces the
 * same pipeline behaviour regardless of what else the user has
 * configured.
 */
export interface Preset {
  _id: string;
  /** `null` for system presets. */
  userId: string | null;
  name: string;
  displayName: string;
  sourceCount: number;
  wordCount: number;
  searchEngines: string[];
  resultsPerPage: number;
  maxResults: number;
  pageLoadTimeout: number;
  maxRetries: number;
  targetReadingGrade: [number, number];
  minKeywordDensity: number;
  maxKeywordDensity: number;
  maxTfidfSimilarity: number;
  maxSemanticSimilarity: number;
  maxAiMarkers: number;
  runReadabilityCheck: boolean;
  runSeoCheck: boolean;
  runOriginalityCheck: boolean;
  runAiMarkerCheck: boolean;
  runGrammarCheck: boolean;
  autoRegenerate: boolean;
  maxRegenerationAttempts: number;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

/**
 * The payload for creating a preset. `userId` is set by the
 * backend (or, for the admin feature, by the service) — the
 * client never supplies it, so it is not part of the payload.
 */
export interface PresetCreatePayload {
  name: string;
  displayName: string;
  sourceCount: number;
  wordCount: number;
  searchEngines: string[];
  resultsPerPage: number;
  maxResults: number;
  pageLoadTimeout: number;
  maxRetries: number;
  targetReadingGrade: [number, number];
  minKeywordDensity: number;
  maxKeywordDensity: number;
  maxTfidfSimilarity: number;
  maxSemanticSimilarity: number;
  maxAiMarkers: number;
  runReadabilityCheck: boolean;
  runSeoCheck: boolean;
  runOriginalityCheck: boolean;
  runAiMarkerCheck: boolean;
  runGrammarCheck: boolean;
  autoRegenerate: boolean;
  maxRegenerationAttempts: number;
}

// ============================================================
//  User settings
// ============================================================

/**
 * The user-controlled preferences that apply across every topic.
 * Per-topic values (keyword list, word count, source count,
 * preset, profile, prompt file) live on the `Topic` document
 * itself; this map only holds the choices that apply when a new
 * topic is created or when a stage completes.
 */
export interface UserSettingsMap {
  defaultPreset: PresetName;
  defaultProfile: ProfileName;
  defaultPromptFile: string;
  autoSaveAfterEachStage: boolean;
  notifyOnCompletion: boolean;
  theme: 'light' | 'dark';
  /**
   * Where the "Download bundle" flow writes the topic's archive
   * ZIP. `'ask'` opens a Save-As dialog on every download so the
   * user picks the location each time; `'default'` writes silently
   * to `bundleDownloadPath`.
   */
  bundleDownloadMode: 'ask' | 'default';
  /**
   * The destination path the bundle flow writes to when
   * `bundleDownloadMode` is `'default'`. Empty string when unset.
   */
  bundleDownloadPath: string;
}

/**
 * The per-user settings document as the desktop receives it from
 * the backend. Exactly one exists per user — the backend
 * upserts one on the first read.
 */
export interface UserSetting {
  _id: string;
  userId: string;
  settings: UserSettingsMap;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}

// ============================================================
//  App version
// ============================================================

/**
 * A published desktop-app release. The most recent record by
 * `createdAt` is the "latest version" that the desktop's update
 * checker compares against its own build version.
 */
export interface AppVersion {
  _id: string;
  version: string;
  downloadUrl: string;
  releaseNotes?: string;
  mandatory?: boolean;
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
}
